Completed means an initial curated cross-reference pass: the complete supplied English source unit and each retained target passage were read, and each pair has a specific explanation. It does not mean exhaustive coverage or independent specialist approval.

Revelation 1:1

A revelation of Jesus Christ, which God gave Him to show His slaves the things that must take place soon. He made it known by sending through His angel to His slave John,

  1. Revelation 22:6 internal cross-reference

    The book's closing repeats its opening formula almost word for word: God “sent His angel to show His slaves the things that must take place soon.” The two verses frame the whole revelation.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 22:6 And he said unto me, “These words are faithful and true, and the Lord, the God of the spirits of the prophets, sent His angel to show His slaves the things that must take place soon.”

    Pinned target record

  2. Revelation 22:16 internal cross-reference

    Both verses describe the revelation as delivered through an angel: in 1:1 it is made known “by sending through His angel to His slave John,” and in 22:16 Jesus says, “I, Jesus, sent My angel to testify these things to you for the Churches.” Revelation 22:16 restates in Jesus’ own voice the chain of transmission set out in the opening verse.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 22:16 “I, Jesus, sent My angel to testify these things to you for the Churches. I am the Root and the offspring of David, the bright morning star.”

    Pinned target record

  3. Daniel 2:28–2:29 verbal allusion

    Daniel speaks of a God in heaven who “disclosed” to the king “what must happen in the last days,” and Revelation opens with what God gave “to show His slaves the things that must take place.” Daniel’s disclosure comes through a dream to a king, whereas Revelation’s comes through an angel to John.

    Read matching passage

    Daniel 2:28 but there is a God in heaven who uncovers mysteries, who has disclosed to King Nebuchadnezzar what must happen in the last days. O king, live for ever! This is the dream and the vision of your head upon your bed:

    Daniel 2:29 You, O king, reclining upon your bed, saw everything that must happen in the last days, and the Revealer of mysteries disclosed to you what must happen.

    Pinned target record

Revelation 1:2

who testified to the word of God and the testimony of Jesus Christ, all that he saw.

  1. Revelation 1:9 internal cross-reference

    Both verses join “the word of God” and “the testimony of Jesus”: in 1:2 as what John testified to, and in 1:9 as the reason John came to be on Patmos.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 1:9 I, John, your brother and fellow partner in the affliction and kingdom and endurance in Jesus, came to be on the island called Patmos because of the word of God and the testimony of Jesus.

    Pinned target record

  2. Revelation 6:9 verbal echo

    The martyrs under the altar were slain “because of the word of God and because of the testimony they held,” the same pairing that describes what John testified to. In Revelation 6:9 the testimony is that held by the martyrs rather than “the testimony of Jesus Christ” by name.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 6:9 And when He opened the fifth seal, I saw beneath the altar the souls of those slaughtered because of the word of God and because of the testimony they held.

    Pinned target record

Revelation 1:3

Blessed is he that readeth, and they that hear the words of the prophecy and keep the things written therein, for the time is near.

  1. Revelation 22:6–22:7 internal cross-reference

    The opening and closing of the book both pronounce a blessing on the one who keeps “the words of the prophecy.” Revelation 22:7 joins this beatitude to the promise “I am coming quickly.”

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 22:6 And he said unto me, “These words are faithful and true, and the Lord, the God of the spirits of the prophets, sent His angel to show His slaves the things that must take place soon.”

    Revelation 22:7 “And behold, I am coming quickly. Blessed is the one who keepeth the words of the prophecy of this scroll.”

    Pinned target record

  2. Revelation 22:10 internal cross-reference

    Both verses speak of “the words of the prophecy” and close with the same reason, “for the time is near.” In Revelation 22:10 the point is that the scroll is not to be sealed.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 22:10 And he saith unto me, “Do not seal the words of the prophecy of this scroll, for the time is near.

    Pinned target record

  3. Luke 11:28 verbal echo

    Both pronounce a blessing on those who hear and keep: “blessed are those who hear the word of God and keep it.” Luke speaks of the word of God generally, Revelation of the words of this prophecy.

    Read matching passage

    Luke 11:28 But He said, “Rather, blessed are those who hear the word of God and keep it!”

    Pinned target record

Revelation 1:4

John, to the seven Churches in Asia: grace to you and peace from the One who is and who was and who is coming, and from the seven spirits before His throne,

  1. Revelation 1:8 same divine title

    Both use the identical threefold title “the One who is and who was and who is coming.” Revelation 1:8 applies it to the Lord God, who says “I am the Alpha and the Omega,” and adds “the Almighty.”

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 1:8 “I am the Alpha and the Omega,” saith the Lord God, the One who is and who was and who is coming, the Almighty.

    Pinned target record

  2. Revelation 4:5 internal cross-reference

    Revelation 1:4 mentions “the seven spirits before His throne,” and Revelation 4:5 sees “seven lamps of fire” burning “before the throne, which are the seven spirits of God.” The later vision gives the image behind the greeting’s phrase.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 4:5 And from the throne come out lightnings and voices and thunders; and seven lamps of fire burn before the throne, which are the seven spirits of God.

    Pinned target record

  3. Exodus 3:14 divine name background

    In the Greek wording followed here, God names Himself to Moses “the One Who Is,” the first element of Revelation’s title for God. Exodus has only this single designation; Revelation extends it with “who was and who is coming.”

    Read matching passage

    Exodus 3:14 And God said to Moses, “I am the One Who Is.” And He said, “This is what you shall say to the sons of Israel: ‘The One Who Is has sent me to you.’”

    Pinned target record

Revelation 1:5

and from Jesus Christ, the faithful witness, the firstborn of the dead and the ruler of the kings of the earth. To the One who loveth us and freed us from our sins in His blood—

  1. Psalms 88:28 verbal allusion

    The Psalm promises, “I will make him firstborn, high among the kings of the earth,” and Revelation calls Jesus “the firstborn of the dead and the ruler of the kings of the earth.” Revelation adds “of the dead,” applying the title to resurrection.

    Read matching passage

    Psalms 88:28 And I will make him firstborn, high among the kings of the earth.

    Pinned target record

  2. Colossians 1:18 same title of Christ

    Both call Christ “the firstborn from the dead” (Revelation: “of the dead”). Colossians links the title to His having “first place in all things,” Revelation to His rule over “the kings of the earth.”

    Read matching passage

    Colossians 1:18 And He is the head of the body, the Church; He is the beginning, the firstborn from the dead, so that He Himself might have first place in all things,

    Pinned target record

  3. 1 John 1:7 shared theme

    Both speak of deliverance from sins by Jesus’ blood: “freed us from our sins in His blood” and “the blood of Jesus His Son cleanses us from every sin.” John’s letter frames this within walking in the light and fellowship.

    Read matching passage

    1 John 1:7 But if we walk in the light, as He is in the light, we have fellowship with one another, and the blood of Jesus His Son cleanses us from every sin.

    Pinned target record

Revelation 1:6

and made us a kingdom, priests to His God and Father—to Him be the glory and dominion for the ages of the ages. Amen.

  1. Revelation 5:10 internal cross-reference

    Both say that God’s people have been made “a kingdom” and “priests” to God (1:6 “made us a kingdom, priests”; 5:10 “Thou didst make them a kingdom and priests”). Revelation 5:10 says this in an address to “Thou” and adds that “they shall reign upon the earth.”

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 5:10 and Thou didst make them a kingdom and priests unto our God, and they shall reign upon the earth.”

    Pinned target record

  2. Exodus 19:6 verbal allusion

    God’s words for the sons of Israel, “you shall be to Me a royal priesthood and a holy nation,” stand behind Revelation’s “a kingdom, priests.” Exodus addresses the sons of Israel; Revelation applies the status to “us,” made a kingdom and priests to His God and Father.

    Read matching passage

    Exodus 19:6 And you shall be to Me a royal priesthood and a holy nation.’ These are the words which you shall speak to the sons of Israel.”

    Pinned target record

  3. 1 Peter 4:11 shared doxology

    Both close with an ascription of “the glory and the dominion” forever, ending in “Amen.” Peter’s ascription follows “so that in all things God may be glorified through Jesus Christ,” while Revelation’s is addressed “to Him” who made us a kingdom, priests to His God and Father.

    Read matching passage

    1 Peter 4:11 If anyone speaks, let it be as words from God; if anyone serves, let it be with the strength God supplies, so that in all things God may be glorified through Jesus Christ, to whom belong the glory and the dominion forever and ever. Amen.

    Pinned target record

Revelation 1:7

Behold, He is coming with the clouds, and every eye will see Him, even those who pierced Him, and all the tribes of the earth will mourn over Him. Yea, amen.

  1. Daniel 7:13 verbal allusion

    Daniel sees “one like a son of man” coming “upon the clouds of heaven,” and Revelation announces, “He is coming with the clouds.” Daniel’s figure is seen in a night vision; Revelation applies the image to a coming seen by “every eye.”

    Read matching passage

    Daniel 7:13 I watched in the night vision, and there, upon the clouds of heaven, one like a son of man was coming. He was present as an Ancient of Days, and those standing by were present with him.

    Pinned target record

  2. Zechariah 12:10 verbal allusion

    Zechariah says that they “will look toward Me” and “mourn over him,” matching Revelation’s seeing and mourning “over Him.” The Greek-based wording here reads “because they danced in mockery,” so the word “pierced” in Revelation is not found in this Zechariah text.

    Read matching passage

    Zechariah 12:10 I will pour upon David’s house and Jerusalem’s residents a spirit of grace and compassion. They will look toward Me because they danced in mockery, and mourn over him as over a beloved one; they will suffer grief as over a firstborn.

    Pinned target record

  3. Matthew 24:30 parallel saying

    Both combine His coming on the clouds (Revelation “with the clouds”; Matthew “upon the clouds of heaven”) with His being seen and the statement that “all the tribes of the earth will mourn.” Matthew speaks of “the Son of Man” and “the sign” in heaven; Revelation adds “those who pierced Him.”

    Read matching passage

    Matthew 24:30 Then the sign of the Son of Man will appear in heaven, and then all the tribes of the earth will mourn; they will see the Son of Man coming upon the clouds of heaven with power and great glory.

    Pinned target record

Revelation 1:8

“I am the Alpha and the Omega,” saith the Lord God, the One who is and who was and who is coming, the Almighty.

  1. Revelation 22:13 same divine title

    Both use the self-designation “I am the Alpha and the Omega.” Revelation 22:13 expands it with “the first and the last, the beginning and the end.”

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 22:13 I am the Alpha and the Omega, the first and the last, the beginning and the end.”

    Pinned target record

  2. Revelation 4:8 same divine title

    Both join “Lord God” and “Almighty” with the threefold title of the One who is, was and is coming. Revelation 4:8 gives the order “who was and who is and who is coming” in the living creatures’ hymn.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 4:8 And the four living creatures, each one of them having six wings, are full of eyes around and within; and they have no rest day and night, saying, “Holy, holy, holy, Lord God Almighty, the One who was and who is and who is coming.”

    Pinned target record

  3. Isaiah 44:6 Old Testament background

    God declares, “I am first, and I am after these things; apart from Me there is no God,” expressing in Isaiah what “the Alpha and the Omega” expresses with the first and last letters. Isaiah does not use the alphabet image.

    Read matching passage

    Isaiah 44:6 Thus says God, Israel’s King, who rescued him, God Sabaoth: “I am first, and I am after these things; apart from Me there is no God.

    Pinned target record

Revelation 1:9

I, John, your brother and fellow partner in the affliction and kingdom and endurance in Jesus, came to be on the island called Patmos because of the word of God and the testimony of Jesus.

  1. Revelation 1:2 internal cross-reference

    Both verses join “the word of God” and “the testimony of Jesus”: Revelation 1:2 describes them as what John testified to, and 1:9 gives the same pair as the reason he was on Patmos.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 1:2 who testified to the word of God and the testimony of Jesus Christ, all that he saw.

    Pinned target record

  2. Acts 14:22 shared theme

    Both join affliction with the kingdom: John is a partner “in the affliction and kingdom,” and Acts teaches that “through many afflictions we must enter the kingdom of God.”

    Read matching passage

    Acts 14:22 strengthening the souls of the disciples, encouraging them to remain in the faith, and saying, “Through many afflictions we must enter the kingdom of God.”

    Pinned target record

  3. Revelation 19:10 verbal echo

    The angel calls himself John’s “fellow slave” and of his “brothers who hold the testimony of Jesus,” echoing John’s description of himself as “brother and fellow partner” because of “the testimony of Jesus.”

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 19:10 And I fell before his feet to worship him. And he saith unto me, “See that thou do it not! I am thy fellow slave and of thy brothers who hold the testimony of Jesus. Worship God, for the testimony of Jesus is the spirit of prophecy.”

    Pinned target record

Revelation 1:10

I came to be in the Spirit on the Lord’s day, and heard behind me a loud voice like a trumpet,

  1. Revelation 4:1–4:2 parallel vision

    Both describe a voice “like a trumpet” speaking to John and his coming to be “in the Spirit.” In Revelation 4:1–2 this leads to the vision of the heavenly throne.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 4:1 After these things I saw, and behold, a door opened in heaven, and the first voice I heard like a trumpet speaking with me said, “Come up hither, and I will show thee what must take place after these things.”

    Revelation 4:2 immediately I came to be in the Spirit; and behold, a throne was set in heaven, and One sitting upon the throne.

    Pinned target record

  2. Revelation 17:3 same visionary formula

    John is “carried me away in the Spirit” to see the woman on the scarlet beast, using the same “in the Spirit” language that opens the first vision. Revelation 17:3 involves being carried to a place, which 1:10 does not mention.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 17:3 And he carried me away in the Spirit into a wilderness. And I saw a woman sitting upon a scarlet beast, full of names of blasphemy, having seven heads and ten horns.

    Pinned target record

  3. Revelation 21:10 same visionary formula

    John is “carried me away in the Spirit” to see the holy city Jerusalem, the same phrase that introduces the first vision. As with 17:3, the later verse adds a transport to a location.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 21:10 And he carried me away in the Spirit upon a great and high mountain, and showed me the holy city Jerusalem coming down out of heaven from God,

    Pinned target record

Revelation 1:11

saying, “What thou seest, write in a scroll and send to the seven Churches: to Ephesus and to Smyrna and to Pergamum and to Thyatira and to Sardis and to Philadelphia and to Laodicea.”

  1. Habakkuk 2:2 prophetic commission to write

    Both are divine commands to write down a vision: “What thou seest, write” and “Write a vision clearly.” Habakkuk names a tablet; Revelation names a scroll to be sent to seven Churches.

    Read matching passage

    Habakkuk 2:2 The Lord answered me: “Write a vision clearly on a boxwood tablet, so that the one reading these things may pursue them.

    Pinned target record

  2. Jeremiah 37:2 prophetic commission to write

    The Lord tells Jeremiah, “Write in a book all the words I delivered to you,” a close parallel to John’s command to “write in a scroll.” Jeremiah records spoken oracles, John what he sees.

    Read matching passage

    Jeremiah 37:2 Thus said the Lord, Israel’s God: Write in a book all the words I delivered to you as oracles,

    Pinned target record

  3. Revelation 1:4 internal cross-reference

    Both name the addressees as “the seven Churches,” 1:4 placing them “in Asia” and 1:11 listing the seven cities by name.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 1:4 John, to the seven Churches in Asia: grace to you and peace from the One who is and who was and who is coming, and from the seven spirits before His throne,

    Pinned target record

Revelation 1:12

And I turned to see the voice that was speaking with me, and having turned, I saw seven golden lampstands,

  1. Zechariah 4:2 shared image

    Zechariah sees a golden lampstand with “seven lamps upon it,” the background for John’s golden lampstands. Zechariah sees one lampstand bearing seven lamps, John “seven golden lampstands.”

    Read matching passage

    Zechariah 4:2 and said to me, “What do you see?” I said, “I see a lampstand entirely of gold, with its lamp-bowl above it, seven lamps upon it, and seven pouring channels for the lamps above it.

    Pinned target record

  2. Revelation 1:20–2:1 interpretation within the vision

    Revelation 1:20 explains “the seven golden lampstands” as “seven Churches,” and 2:1 describes Christ as walking “in the midst of the seven golden lampstands.”

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 1:20 The mystery of the seven stars thou sawest upon My right hand, and the seven golden lampstands: the seven stars are angels of the seven Churches, and the seven lampstands are seven Churches.”

    Revelation 2:1 “To the angel of the Church in Ephesus write: These things saith the One who holdeth the seven stars in His right hand, who walketh in the midst of the seven golden lampstands:

    Pinned target record

  3. Revelation 1:13 continuation of the vision

    The next verse completes the scene John turned to see: “in the midst of the lampstands One like a son of man.”

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 1:13 and in the midst of the lampstands One like a son of man, clothed in a robe reaching to the feet and girded around the chest with a golden belt.

    Pinned target record

Revelation 1:13

and in the midst of the lampstands One like a son of man, clothed in a robe reaching to the feet and girded around the chest with a golden belt.

  1. Daniel 7:13 same title

    Both use the description “one like a son of man.” Daniel’s figure comes upon the clouds of heaven; Revelation’s stands among the lampstands.

    Read matching passage

    Daniel 7:13 I watched in the night vision, and there, upon the clouds of heaven, one like a son of man was coming. He was present as an Ancient of Days, and those standing by were present with him.

    Pinned target record

  2. Daniel 10:5–10:6 shared image

    Daniel sees “a man clothed in fine linen” with linen “wrapped around his waist,” comparable to the figure “clothed in a robe” and “girded around the chest with a golden belt.” Daniel places the girding at the waist and does not mention gold.

    Read matching passage

    Daniel 10:5 and I lifted my eyes and saw a man clothed in fine linen, with fine linen wrapped around his waist, and light coming from his middle.

    Daniel 10:6 His body was like a tarshish stone, his face like a flash of lightning, his eyes like flaming torches, his arms and feet like flashing bronze, and the sound of his speech like the sound of an uproar.

    Pinned target record

  3. Revelation 15:6 verbal echo

    The seven angels are “girded around their chests with golden belts,” the same attire described for the One like a son of man.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 15:6 and the seven angels having the seven plagues came out of the temple, clothed in pure, bright linen and girded around their chests with golden belts.

    Pinned target record

Revelation 1:14

And His head and hair were white like white wool, like snow, and His eyes like a flame of fire,

  1. Daniel 7:9 shared image

    Daniel describes the Ancient of Days with a covering “like snow” and hair “like pure white wool,” with a throne “like a flame of fire.” Revelation applies white wool and snow to the head and hair and the flame of fire to the eyes.

    Read matching passage

    Daniel 7:9 I watched until thrones were set in place and an Ancient of Days was seated, wearing a covering like snow, with the hair of His head like pure white wool. The throne was like a flame of fire,

    Pinned target record

  2. Daniel 10:6 shared image

    Daniel’s heavenly man has “eyes like flaming torches,” comparable to “eyes like a flame of fire.”

    Read matching passage

    Daniel 10:6 His body was like a tarshish stone, his face like a flash of lightning, his eyes like flaming torches, his arms and feet like flashing bronze, and the sound of his speech like the sound of an uproar.

    Pinned target record

  3. Revelation 2:18 internal cross-reference

    The letter to Thyatira introduces the Son of God as having “His eyes like a flame of fire,” repeating this feature of the opening vision.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 2:18 And to the angel of the Church in Thyatira write: These things saith the Son of God, who hath His eyes like a flame of fire, and His feet like burnished bronze:

    Pinned target record

Revelation 1:15

and His feet like burnished bronze, as refined in a furnace, and His voice like the sound of many waters.

  1. Revelation 2:18 internal cross-reference

    The letter to Thyatira repeats “His feet like burnished bronze” from the opening vision.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 2:18 And to the angel of the Church in Thyatira write: These things saith the Son of God, who hath His eyes like a flame of fire, and His feet like burnished bronze:

    Pinned target record

  2. Daniel 10:6 shared image

    Daniel’s heavenly man has “arms and feet like flashing bronze” and speech like “the sound of an uproar,” paralleling the bronze feet and overwhelming voice. Daniel does not use the phrase “many waters.”

    Read matching passage

    Daniel 10:6 His body was like a tarshish stone, his face like a flash of lightning, his eyes like flaming torches, his arms and feet like flashing bronze, and the sound of his speech like the sound of an uproar.

    Pinned target record

  3. Psalms 92:4 verbal echo

    The Psalm speaks of “the voices of many waters” with the Lord wondrous on high, matching “the sound of many waters.” In the Psalm the waters are the sea, not the Lord’s voice itself.

    Read matching passage

    Psalms 92:4 From the voices of many waters, wondrous are the sea’s surges; wondrous on high is the Lord.

    Pinned target record

Revelation 1:16

And He held seven stars in His right hand, and from His mouth a sharp two-edged sword was coming out, and His appearance was like the sun shining in its strength.

  1. Revelation 2:12 internal cross-reference

    The letter to Pergamum introduces Christ as “the One who hath the sharp two-edged sword,” repeating this feature of the opening vision.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 2:12 And to the angel of the Church in Pergamum write: These things saith the One who hath the sharp two-edged sword:

    Pinned target record

  2. Isaiah 49:2 shared image

    The speaker in Isaiah says that God “made my mouth like a sharp sword,” the image behind the sharp two-edged sword coming from His mouth in Revelation. In Isaiah the mouth is compared to a sword; in Revelation a sword comes out of it.

    Read matching passage

    Isaiah 49:2 and made my mouth like a sharp sword. In the shelter of His hand He hid me; He made me a chosen arrow and sheltered me in His quiver.

    Pinned target record

  3. Matthew 17:2 shared image

    At the transfiguration Jesus’ face “shone like the sun,” comparable to His appearance “like the sun shining in its strength.”

    Read matching passage

    Matthew 17:2 He was transfigured before them. His face shone like the sun, and His garments became white like the light.

    Pinned target record

Revelation 1:17

And when I saw Him, I fell at His feet as dead. And He placed His right hand upon me, saying, “Fear not. I am the first and the last,

  1. Isaiah 44:6 divine self-designation

    God says, “I am first, and I am after these things,” the background to “I am the first and the last.” Isaiah’s wording uses “after these things” rather than “the last.”

    Read matching passage

    Isaiah 44:6 Thus says God, Israel’s King, who rescued him, God Sabaoth: “I am first, and I am after these things; apart from Me there is no God.

    Pinned target record

  2. Revelation 2:8 internal cross-reference

    The letter to Smyrna introduces Christ as “the first and the last, who became dead and lived,” repeating the self-designation of 1:17 together with 1:18.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 2:8 And to the angel of the Church in Smyrna write: These things saith the first and the last, who became dead and lived:

    Pinned target record

  3. Ezekiel 1:28 parallel response to vision

    Ezekiel, seeing the likeness of the Lord’s glory, falls upon his face and hears a voice speaking, as John falls “at His feet as dead” and is spoken to. Ezekiel is not touched by a hand in this verse.

    Read matching passage

    Ezekiel 1:28 Like the appearance of a bow when it is in the cloud on a rainy day, so was the arrangement of the brightness all around. This was the appearance of the likeness of the Lord’s glory. I saw it, and I fall upon my face, and I heard the voice of One speaking.

    Pinned target record

Revelation 1:18

and the living One. I became dead, and behold, I am living for the ages of the ages, and I have the keys of death and Hades.

  1. Romans 6:9 shared theme

    Both state that Christ died and now lives without death having power over Him: “no longer dies; death no longer rules over Him,” and “I became dead, and behold, I am living for the ages of the ages.”

    Read matching passage

    Romans 6:9 knowing that Christ, having been raised from the dead, no longer dies; death no longer rules over Him.

    Pinned target record

  2. Wisdom of Solomon 16:13 shared image

    Wisdom ascribes to God “authority over life and death” and the power to bring down to and up from “the gates of Hades,” comparable to Christ’s “keys of death and Hades.” Wisdom speaks of gates, Revelation of keys.

    Read matching passage

    Wisdom of Solomon 16:13 For Thou hast authority over life and death, and Thou bringest down to the gates of Hades and bringest up again;

    Pinned target record

  3. Revelation 20:14 internal cross-reference

    “Death and Hades” appear again as a pair when they are “thrown into the lake of fire,” showing the end of the powers whose keys Christ holds.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 20:14 And Death and Hades were thrown into the lake of fire. This is the second death, the lake of fire.

    Pinned target record

Revelation 1:19

Therefore write the things thou sawest, and the things that are, and the things that are to take place after these.

  1. Revelation 1:11 internal cross-reference

    The command “write the things thou sawest” repeats the first commission in 1:11, “What thou seest, write in a scroll.” 1:19 adds the things that are and the things to take place after these.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 1:11 saying, “What thou seest, write in a scroll and send to the seven Churches: to Ephesus and to Smyrna and to Pergamum and to Thyatira and to Sardis and to Philadelphia and to Laodicea.”

    Pinned target record

Revelation 1:20

The mystery of the seven stars thou sawest upon My right hand, and the seven golden lampstands: the seven stars are angels of the seven Churches, and the seven lampstands are seven Churches.”

  1. Revelation 2:1 internal cross-reference

    The first letter introduces Christ as holding “the seven stars in His right hand” and walking among “the seven golden lampstands,” the two symbols explained in 1:20.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 2:1 “To the angel of the Church in Ephesus write: These things saith the One who holdeth the seven stars in His right hand, who walketh in the midst of the seven golden lampstands:

    Pinned target record

  2. Revelation 1:16 internal cross-reference

    Revelation 1:16 is the description being explained: “He held seven stars in His right hand.”

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 1:16 And He held seven stars in His right hand, and from His mouth a sharp two-edged sword was coming out, and His appearance was like the sun shining in its strength.

    Pinned target record

  3. Zechariah 4:2 shared image

    Zechariah’s golden lampstand with “seven lamps upon it” stands behind the seven golden lampstands. Zechariah has one lampstand, and the explanation as Churches is Revelation’s own.

    Read matching passage

    Zechariah 4:2 and said to me, “What do you see?” I said, “I see a lampstand entirely of gold, with its lamp-bowl above it, seven lamps upon it, and seven pouring channels for the lamps above it.

Revelation 2:1

“To the angel of the Church in Ephesus write: These things saith the One who holdeth the seven stars in His right hand, who walketh in the midst of the seven golden lampstands:

  1. Revelation 1:20 internal cross-reference

    Both mention the seven stars in the right hand and “the seven golden lampstands”; 1:20 explains them as angels of the Churches and the Churches themselves.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 1:20 The mystery of the seven stars thou sawest upon My right hand, and the seven golden lampstands: the seven stars are angels of the seven Churches, and the seven lampstands are seven Churches.”

Revelation 2:2

‘I know thy works, and the labour and thine endurance, and that thou canst not bear evil people; and thou didst test those who call themselves apostles and are not, and didst find them false.

  1. 2 Corinthians 11:13–11:15 shared theme

    Both concern people who claim to be apostles falsely: Paul speaks of “false apostles” disguising themselves “as apostles of Christ,” and Ephesus tested “those who call themselves apostles and are not.”

    Read matching passage

    2 Corinthians 11:13 For such people are false apostles, deceitful workers, disguising themselves as apostles of Christ.

    2 Corinthians 11:14 And no wonder, for Satan himself disguises himself as an angel of light.

    2 Corinthians 11:15 Therefore it is no surprise if his ministers also disguise themselves as ministers of righteousness; their end will be according to their works.

    Pinned target record

  2. 1 John 4:1 shared theme

    Both commend testing claimants: “test the spirits to see whether they are from God,” and “thou didst test those who call themselves apostles.” John’s letter speaks of false prophets rather than false apostles.

    Read matching passage

    1 John 4:1 Beloved, do not believe every spirit, but test the spirits to see whether they are from God, because many false prophets have gone out into the world.

    Pinned target record

  3. Revelation 2:9 parallel construction

    The letter to Smyrna uses the same pattern of false self-description, “those who say that they themselves are Jews and are not,” as Ephesus’s “those who call themselves apostles and are not.”

    Read matching passage

Revelation 2:3

And thou hast endurance and didst bear up for My name’s sake, and hast not grown weary.

Revelation 2:4

But I have this against thee: thou didst abandon thy first love.

  1. Jeremiah 2:2–2:5 Old Testament background

    The Lord remembers Israel’s early “love of your completion” and asks why their fathers “withdrew far from Me,” comparable to the charge “thou didst abandon thy first love.” Jeremiah addresses Israel, Revelation a Church.

    Read matching passage

    Jeremiah 2:2 And He said, Thus says the Lord: I remembered the mercy of your youth and the love of your completion, when you followed the Holy One of Israel, says the Lord.

    Jeremiah 2:3 Israel is holy. To the Lord he is the first of His produce. All who devour him will offend; calamities will come upon them, declares the Lord.

    Jeremiah 2:4 Hear the Lord’s word, house of Jacob and every clan of the house of Israel.

    Jeremiah 2:5 Thus says the Lord: What offense did your fathers find in Me, that they withdrew far from Me, followed futile things and became futile?

    Pinned target record

  2. Matthew 24:12–24:13 shared theme

    Jesus warns that “the love of many will grow cold,” a decline of love comparable to the abandoned first love. Matthew ties this to increasing lawlessness, which Revelation 2:4 does not mention.

    Read matching passage

    Matthew 24:12 Because lawlessness increases, the love of many will grow cold.

    Matthew 24:13 But the one who endures to the end will be saved.

    Pinned target record

  1. Revelation 2:16 same formula

    Both call for repentance with a conditional threat: “Therefore repent. Otherwise I am coming to thee.” At Pergamum the threat is war with the sword of His mouth; at Ephesus removal of the lampstand.

    Revelation 3:3 Therefore remember how thou hast received and heard, and keep it and repent. Therefore if thou watch not, I will come like a thief, and thou shalt certainly not know at what hour I will come upon thee.

    Read matching passage

  2. Revelation 3:17–3:18 internal contrast

    Smyrna is poor “but thou art rich,” whereas Laodicea says “I am rich” but is “poor.” The two letters reverse the same contrast of riches and poverty.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 3:17 Because thou sayest, “I am rich and have become wealthy and need nothing,” and knowest not that thou art the wretched and pitiable and poor and blind and naked one,

    Revelation 3:18 I counsel thee to buy from Me gold refined by fire, that thou mayest become rich, and white garments, that thou mayest clothe thyself and the shame of thy nakedness may not be manifested, and eye salve to anoint thine eyes, that thou mayest see.

    James says God chose “those poor in the world to be rich in faith,” matching the paradox “poverty—but thou art rich.”

    James 2:6 But you have dishonored the poor person. Is it not the rich who oppress you and drag you into courts?

    The “sword of My mouth” recalls the opening vision, in which “from His mouth a sharp two-edged sword was coming out.”

    In Revelation 19:15 “from His mouth cometh out a sharp sword, so that with it He may strike the nations,” the same image of judgment with the sword of His mouth threatened here. In 2:16 the threat concerns those in Pergamum; in 19:15 the sword strikes the nations.

    Read matching passage

    The figure in Isaiah will “strike the earth with the word of his mouth,” and “with breath through his lips he will slay the ungodly,” the background for judgment by what comes from the mouth. Isaiah does not speak of a sword.

    Read matching passage

    Isaiah 11:4 but he will judge the cause of the lowly, and reprove the lowly of the earth; and he will strike the earth with the word of his mouth, and with breath through his lips he will slay the ungodly.

    Wisdom says that in judging gradually God “didst give room for repentance” to those who would not change, as Christ “gave her time to repent.”

    Read matching passage

  3. Romans 2:4–2:5 shared theme

    Paul speaks of God’s forbearance meant to lead to “repentance” and of an “unrepentant heart” that stores up wrath, comparable to time given and repentance refused.

Revelation 2:22

Behold, I am throwing her into a bed, and those committing adultery with her into great affliction, unless they repent of her works.

  1. Ezekiel 23:45–23:48 Old Testament background

    Ezekiel announces punishment “of an adulteress” and says “All women will be disciplined,” comparable to judgment on Jezebel and on those committing adultery with her. Ezekiel’s penalties are stoning and the sword rather than a bed and affliction.

    Read matching passage

    Ezekiel 23:45 Righteous men will punish them with the punishment of an adulteress and the punishment of bloodshed, for they are adulteresses, and blood is on their hands.

    Ezekiel 23:46 Thus says the Lord, the Lord: Bring a multitude against them and set turmoil and plunder among them,

    Ezekiel 23:47 and stone them with the multitude’s stones and pierce them with their swords. They will kill their sons and daughters and burn their houses.

    Ezekiel 23:48 I will turn impiety away from the land. All women will be disciplined and certainly will not act according to their impieties.

    Pinned target record

  2. Ezekiel 16:37–16:41 Old Testament background

    God gathers against the unfaithful one “all your lovers” and will exact “the retribution of an adulteress,” comparable to the judgment on Jezebel and those committing adultery with her. In Ezekiel the lovers are gathered as agents of the punishment, whereas in Revelation those committing adultery with her share in the affliction.

    Read matching passage

    Ezekiel 16:37 Therefore behold, I am gathering against you all your lovers with whom you mingled, all whom you loved together with all whom you hated. I will gather them against you from all around, reveal your evils to them, and they will see all your shame.

    Ezekiel 16:38 I will exact retribution upon you with the retribution of an adulteress and a woman shedding blood, and place you in blood of wrath and jealousy.

    Ezekiel 16:39 I will hand you into their hands. They will tear down your brothel, pull down your platform, strip your clothing from you, take your vessels of boasting, and leave you naked and exposed.

    Ezekiel 16:40 They will bring crowds against you, stone you with stones, and slaughter you with their swords.

    Ezekiel 16:41 They will burn your houses with fire and exact retributions upon you before many women. I will turn you away from your prostitution, and you certainly will give no more payments.

    Pinned target record

  3. Revelation 18:9 shared image

    The kings of the earth who “committed sexual immorality and lived in luxury with her” will “weep and mourn over her” when they see her burning; as in 2:22, those who shared a woman’s immorality are caught up in grief at her judgment. Revelation 18:9 does not name the woman and speaks of mourning rather than of affliction imposed on them.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 18:9 And the kings of the earth who committed sexual immorality and lived in luxury with her will weep and mourn over her when they see the smoke of her burning,

    Pinned target record

Revelation 2:23

And I will kill her children with death, and all the Churches will know that I am the One who searcheth kidneys and hearts, and I will give to each of you according to your works.

  1. Jeremiah 17:10 verbal allusion

    The Lord says, “I, the Lord, examine hearts and test kidneys, to give each according to his ways,” combining both elements of Revelation 2:23: searching “kidneys and hearts” and giving “to each of you according to your works.”

    Read matching passage

    Jeremiah 17:10 I, the Lord, examine hearts and test kidneys, to give each according to his ways and the fruits of his practices.

    The Psalm names God as the One “who tests hearts and kidneys,” the title Christ claims as “the One who searcheth kidneys and hearts.”

    Read matching passage

    The Son of Man “will repay each person according to his conduct,” matching “I will give to each of you according to your works.”

Revelation 2:24

But to you, the rest in Thyatira, as many as do not have this teaching, who have not known the deep things of Satan, as they say, I say: I place no other burden upon you.

  1. Acts 15:28 verbal echo

    The Jerusalem letter says “to place no further burden upon you,” close to “I place no other burden upon you.” Acts attributes the decision to “the Holy Spirit and to us,” while in Revelation the speaker says, “I say.”

    Read matching passage

    Acts 15:28 For it seemed good to the Holy Spirit and to us to place no further burden upon you except these necessary things:

  2. 1 Corinthians 2:10 contrasting phrase

    Paul speaks of “the depths of God” revealed through the Spirit, a counterpart to “the deep things of Satan” claimed by the false teachers.

    Read matching passage

    1 Corinthians 2:10 But God revealed them to us through the Spirit, for the Spirit searches everything, even the depths of God.

Only hold fast what ye have until I come.

  1. Psalms 2:8 verbal allusion

    The Psalm’s promise, “I will give you nations as your inheritance,” lies behind “I will give authority over the nations.” In the Psalm the promise is made to the one addressed as “you”; Revelation extends it to the conqueror.

    Read matching passage

    Psalms 2:8 Ask of Me, and I will give you nations as your inheritance, and the ends of the earth as your possession.

  2. Daniel 7:27 shared theme

    Daniel says “authority” and rule over kingdoms are given “to a holy people of the Most High,” comparable to authority over the nations given to the faithful conqueror.

    Read matching passage

Revelation 2:27

and he will shepherd them with an iron rod, as vessels of pottery are shattered,

  1. Psalms 2:8–2:9 quotation

    Revelation quotes Psalm 2:9: “You will shepherd them with an iron rod; you will crush them like a potter’s vessel.” Revelation applies to the conqueror the promise made in the Psalm to the one who is given “nations as your inheritance.”

    Read matching passage

    Psalms 2:8 Ask of Me, and I will give you nations as your inheritance, and the ends of the earth as your possession.

    Psalms 2:9 You will shepherd them with an iron rod; you will crush them like a potter’s vessel.’”

    In Revelation 19:15 the One from whose mouth comes a sharp sword “will shepherd them with an iron rod,” the same phrase used here of the conqueror’s rule.

Revelation 3:1

And to the angel of the Church in Sardis write: These things saith the One who hath the seven spirits of God and the seven stars: ‘I know thy works, that thou hast a name that thou livest, and thou art dead.

    Paul says that the self-indulgent woman “is dead while living,” paralleling the Church that has “a name that thou livest, and thou art dead.”

Revelation 3:2

Become watchful, and strengthen the things remaining that were about to die, for I have not found thy works fulfilled before My God.

  1. Revelation 16:15 shared warning

    Both call for watchfulness: Sardis is told, “Become watchful,” and Revelation 16:15 says, “Blessed is the one who watcheth.” Revelation 16:15 grounds this in “I am coming like a thief” and adds keeping one’s garments, which the Sardis verse does not mention.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 16:15 “Behold, I am coming like a thief. Blessed is the one who watcheth and keepeth his garments, so that he may not walk naked and they see his shame.”

    Pinned target record

  2. Mark 13:33–13:37 shared warning

    Jesus’ repeated command to “keep watch,” lest the master come and find them sleeping, parallels “Become watchful” addressed to a Church whose works were not found complete.

    Read matching passage

    Mark 13:33 Watch out; stay awake, for you do not know when the time is.

    Mark 13:34 It is like a man going abroad who left his house and gave his slaves authority, each his own work, and commanded the doorkeeper to keep watch.

    Mark 13:35 Therefore keep watch, for you do not know when the master of the house is coming: in the evening, at midnight, at cockcrow, or in the morning.

    Mark 13:36 Otherwise he may come suddenly and find you sleeping.

    Mark 13:37 What I say to you, I say to all: keep watch!”

    Pinned target record

Revelation 3:3

Therefore remember how thou hast received and heard, and keep it and repent. Therefore if thou watch not, I will come like a thief, and thou shalt certainly not know at what hour I will come upon thee.

  1. Revelation 16:15 internal cross-reference

    Both sayings have the Lord announcing that he will come “like a thief” and link this with watching. Revelation 16:15 adds a blessing on the one who keeps his garments, whereas the Sardis warning is conditional: “if thou watch not.”

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 16:15 “Behold, I am coming like a thief. Blessed is the one who watcheth and keepeth his garments, so that he may not walk naked and they see his shame.”

    Pinned target record

  2. Matthew 25:13 verbal allusion

    Both pair a call to watch with ignorance of the time of the coming: Sardis “shalt certainly not know at what hour,” and Matthew’s hearers “know neither the day nor the hour.”

    Read matching passage

    Matthew 25:13 Therefore keep watch, because you know neither the day nor the hour.

    Pinned target record

  3. 1 Thessalonians 5:2 shared image

    Both use the image of coming “like a thief.” Paul applies it to “the day of the Lord,” while Revelation 3:3 has the speaker say “I will come like a thief” as a conditional warning.

    Read matching passage

    1 Thessalonians 5:2 For you yourselves know exactly that the day of the Lord comes in this way, like a thief in the night.

    Pinned target record

Revelation 3:4

But thou hast a few names in Sardis who did not defile their garments, and they shall walk with Me in white, because they are worthy.

  1. Revelation 3:5 internal cross-reference

    The next verse continues the promise: those who “walk with Me in white” correspond to the conqueror who will be “clothed in white garments.”

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 3:5 The one who conquereth will thus be clothed in white garments, and I will certainly not erase his name from the book of life, and I will confess his name before My Father and before His angels.

    Pinned target record

  2. Revelation 3:18 shared image

    Both connect white clothing with standing before the Lord: the few in Sardis “did not defile their garments” and “shall walk with Me in white,” and Laodicea is counselled to buy from Him “white garments” so that “the shame of thy nakedness may not be manifested.”

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 3:18 I counsel thee to buy from Me gold refined by fire, that thou mayest become rich, and white garments, that thou mayest clothe thyself and the shame of thy nakedness may not be manifested, and eye salve to anoint thine eyes, that thou mayest see.

    Pinned target record

  3. Jude 1:23 shared image

    Both use soiled clothing as an image of moral defilement: Sardis’ faithful “did not defile their garments,” and Jude speaks of “the tunic stained by the flesh.”

    Read matching passage

    Jude 1:23 save others, snatching them out of the fire; and have mercy on others with fear, hating even the tunic stained by the flesh.

Revelation 3:5

The one who conquereth will thus be clothed in white garments, and I will certainly not erase his name from the book of life, and I will confess his name before My Father and before His angels.

  1. Psalms 68:29 verbal allusion

    Both speak of names being erased from a book of life: the Psalm asks that the wicked “be erased from the book of the living,” while Revelation promises the conqueror, “I will certainly not erase his name from the book of life.”

    Read matching passage

    Psalms 68:29 Let them be erased from the book of the living, and let them not be written with the righteous.

    Pinned target record

  2. Matthew 10:32 parallel saying

    Both promise that Jesus will acknowledge or confess the faithful person “before My Father.” Matthew conditions this on acknowledging Jesus before people; Revelation on conquering.

    Read matching passage

    Matthew 10:32 Therefore everyone who acknowledges Me before people, I too will acknowledge before My Father in the heavens.

  3. Luke 12:8 parallel saying

    Both promise acknowledgement before the angels: Luke says the Son of Man will acknowledge “before the angels of God,” and Revelation “before His angels.”

    Read matching passage

    Luke 12:8 And I tell you, everyone who acknowledges Me before people, the Son of Man will also acknowledge before the angels of God.

Let the one who hath an ear hear what the Spirit saith to the Churches.’

    Both verses contain the identical refrain “Let the one who hath an ear hear what the Spirit saith to the Churches.” In Revelation 2:7 it is followed by a promise to the conqueror.

Revelation 3:7

And to the angel of the Church in Philadelphia write: These things saith the Holy One, the True One, the One who hath the key of David, who openeth and no one will shut, and shutteth and no one openeth:

  1. Revelation 6:10 same divine titles

    The speaker to Philadelphia is “the Holy One, the True One,” and the martyrs address God as “Master, holy and true.” Revelation 6:10 uses the pair as an address in prayer rather than as a self-description.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 6:10 And they cried with a loud voice, saying, “How long, O Master, holy and true, before Thou dost judge and avenge our blood upon those dwelling on the earth?”

    Pinned target record

  2. Revelation 1:18 same figure holding keys

    Both describe Christ as holding a key or keys: “the key of David” here, and “the keys of death and Hades” in the inaugural vision.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 1:18 and the living One. I became dead, and behold, I am living for the ages of the ages, and I have the keys of death and Hades.

    Pinned target record

  3. Isaiah 22:22 Old Testament background

    Both describe authority connected with David that cannot be opposed: Isaiah’s figure receives “David’s glory” and rules “with no one contradicting,” and Christ opens and “no one will shut.” The Saints (Greek) wording of Isaiah has “David’s glory” rather than a key and does not mention opening and shutting.

    Read matching passage

    Isaiah 22:22 I will give him David’s glory, and he will rule, with no one contradicting.

    Pinned target record

Revelation 3:8

    The “opened door, which no one can shut” applies the title of the previous verse, the One who “openeth and no one will shut.”

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 3:7 And to the angel of the Church in Philadelphia write: These things saith the Holy One, the True One, the One who hath the key of David, who openeth and no one will shut, and shutteth and no one openeth:

  2. Revelation 2:13 parallel commendation

    Both churches are commended for not denying under pressure: Philadelphia “didst not deny My name,” and Pergamum “holdest fast My name and didst not deny My faith.”

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 2:13 ‘I know where thou dwellest, where Satan’s throne is, and thou holdest fast My name and didst not deny My faith, even in the days of Antipas, My witness, My faithful one, who was killed among you, where Satan dwelleth.

    Pinned target record

  3. John 17:6 verbal echo

    Both commend those who have kept the word: Philadelphia “didst keep My word,” and Jesus says of the people the Father gave Him, “they have kept Thy word,” in the context of revealing “Thy name.”

    Read matching passage

    John 17:6 “I revealed Thy name to the people whom Thou gavest Me out of the world. They were Thine, and Thou gavest them to Me, and they have kept Thy word.

Revelation 3:9

Behold, I give some from the synagogue of Satan, those who say that they themselves are Jews and are not, but lie. Behold, I will make them come and bow before thy feet, and know that I loved thee.

  1. Revelation 2:9 internal cross-reference

    Both letters describe the same group in nearly the same words: “those who say that they themselves are Jews and are not” and “a synagogue of Satan.”

    Read matching passage

  2. Isaiah 49:23 verbal allusion

    Both describe outsiders who will “bow” at the feet of those addressed, followed by recognition: Isaiah’s “You will know that I am the Lord” and Revelation’s “know that I loved thee.” In Isaiah it is kings and ruling women who bow and “lick the dust of your feet.”

    Read matching passage

    Isaiah 49:23 and kings will be your foster fathers and ruling women your nurses. They will bow to you upon the earth’s surface and lick the dust of your feet. You will know that I am the Lord, and you will not be ashamed.

    Pinned target record

  3. Isaiah 43:4 verbal allusion

    Both have God declaring his love for his people with “I loved”: Isaiah “I loved you,” and Revelation, that opponents will “know that I loved thee.”

    Read matching passage

Revelation 3:10

Because thou didst keep the word of My endurance, I also will keep thee from the hour of trial that is about to come upon the whole inhabited world, to try those dwelling upon the earth.

  1. 2 Peter 2:9 parallel promise

    Both speak of the Lord keeping or rescuing the faithful from trial: Peter says the Lord knows how “to rescue the godly from trial,” and Revelation promises, “I also will keep thee from the hour of trial.”

    2 Peter 2:9 then the Lord knows how to rescue the godly from trial, and to keep the unrighteous under punishment for the day of judgment,

    Both letters speak of a coming testing that is “about to” happen, Smyrna being told that the devil is about to imprison some “that ye may be tested.” Smyrna is told to endure the testing, whereas Philadelphia is promised to be kept from the hour of trial.

    Revelation 2:10 Fear none of the things thou art about to suffer. Behold, the devil is about to throw some of you into prison, that ye may be tested, and ye shall have affliction for ten days. Become faithful until death, and I will give thee the crown of life.

Revelation 3:15

    Both letters open their verdict with “I know thy works, that thou…” and then expose a hidden condition: Sardis “thou art dead,” Laodicea “neither cold nor hot.”

Revelation 3:16

So because thou art lukewarm, and neither hot nor cold, I am about to vomit thee out of My mouth.

  1. Revelation 3:15 internal cross-reference

    3:15 states the charge, “thou art neither cold nor hot,” and 3:16 draws the consequence for the one who is “lukewarm, and neither hot nor cold”: being vomited out of the Lord’s mouth.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 3:15 ‘I know thy works, that thou art neither cold nor hot. Would that thou wert cold or hot.

Revelation 3:17

Because thou sayest, “I am rich and have become wealthy and need nothing,” and knowest not that thou art the wretched and pitiable and poor and blind and naked one,

  1. Hosea 12:9 verbal allusion

    Both quote a self-satisfied claim to wealth: Ephraim says, “Indeed I have become rich,” and Laodicea says, “I am rich and have become wealthy.” Hosea adds that his labours will not be found because of his injustices.

    Read matching passage

    Hosea 12:9 Ephraim said, ‘Indeed I have become rich; I have found refreshment for myself.’ All his labors will not be found for him because of the injustices in which he sinned.

    Pinned target record

  2. 1 Corinthians 4:8–4:10 verbal echo

    Both expose complacent self-assessment of wealth: Paul ironically tells the Corinthians, “Already you have become rich!” and Laodicea says, “I am rich and have become wealthy and need nothing.”

    Read matching passage

    1 Corinthians 4:8 Already you have your fill! Already you have become rich! Without us you have become kings! And I wish indeed that you had become kings, so that we too might reign with you.

    1 Corinthians 4:9 For I think God has displayed us apostles last of all, as people condemned to death, because we have become a spectacle to the world, both to angels and to human beings.

    1 Corinthians 4:10 We are foolish for Christ’s sake, but you are wise in Christ. We are weak, but you are strong. You are honored, but we are dishonored.

    Pinned target record

  3. John 9:40–9:41 shared image

    Both expose people who think they see while being blind: the Pharisees say “We see,” and Laodicea “knowest not that thou art… blind.”

    Read matching passage

    John 9:40 Some of the Pharisees who were with Him heard these things and said to Him, “Are we also blind?”

    John 9:41 Jesus said to them, “If you were blind, you would not have sin. But now you say, ‘We see’; your sin remains.”

    Pinned target record

Revelation 3:18

I counsel thee to buy from Me gold refined by fire, that thou mayest become rich, and white garments, that thou mayest clothe thyself and the shame of thy nakedness may not be manifested, and eye salve to anoint thine eyes, that thou mayest see.

  1. Revelation 3:17 internal cross-reference

    The counsel answers the preceding diagnosis point by point: gold for the one who is “poor,” white garments for the “naked,” and eye salve for the “blind.”

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 3:17 Because thou sayest, “I am rich and have become wealthy and need nothing,” and knowest not that thou art the wretched and pitiable and poor and blind and naked one,

    Pinned target record

  2. Revelation 16:15 shared image

    Both link garments with avoiding the shame of nakedness: Laodicea is to clothe itself so that “the shame of thy nakedness may not be manifested,” and Revelation 16:15 blesses the one who keeps his garments “so that he may not walk naked and they see his shame.”

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 16:15 “Behold, I am coming like a thief. Blessed is the one who watcheth and keepeth his garments, so that he may not walk naked and they see his shame.”

    Pinned target record

  3. 1 Peter 1:7 shared image

    Both use gold tested or “refined by fire” as an image of value. Peter compares tested faith to such gold, whereas Revelation offers the gold itself as a purchase from the Lord.

    Read matching passage

    1 Peter 1:7 so that the tested genuineness of your faith—more precious than gold that perishes, though tested by fire—may be found to result in praise and glory and honor at the revelation of Jesus Christ.

    Pinned target record

Revelation 3:19

As many as I love, I reprove and discipline. Therefore be zealous and repent.

  1. Proverbs 3:11–3:12 verbal allusion

    Both state that discipline comes from love: “As many as I love, I reprove and discipline,” and “the Lord disciplines the one He loves.” Proverbs also speaks of being “corrected by Him.”

    Read matching passage

    Proverbs 3:11 Son, do not make light of the Lord’s discipline or lose heart when corrected by Him,

    Proverbs 3:12 for the Lord disciplines the one He loves and scourges every son He receives.

    Pinned target record

  2. Job 5:17 verbal echo

    Both present the Lord’s reproof as something to accept: Job calls happy “the person whom the Lord reproved,” and Christ reproves those he loves.

    Read matching passage

    Job 5:17 Happy is the person whom the Lord reproved; do not refuse the Almighty’s admonition.

    Pinned target record

  3. Deuteronomy 8:5 shared image

    Both describe the Lord’s discipline as an expression of a close relationship: Deuteronomy compares it to how “a man chastens his son,” while Revelation grounds it in love.

    Read matching passage

    Deuteronomy 8:5 And you shall know in your heart that, as a man chastens his son, so shall the Lord your God chasten you.

    Pinned target record

Revelation 3:20

Behold, I stand at the door and knock. If anyone hear My voice and open the door, I will come in unto him and dine with him, and he with Me.

  1. Luke 12:36–12:37 parallel image

    Both picture someone who “knocks” and those inside who should “open” to him, followed by a shared meal: Luke’s master has his slaves “recline at table,” and Christ will “dine with him.”

    Read matching passage

    Luke 12:36 and be like people waiting for their own master to return from the wedding feast, so that when he comes and knocks, they may open to him at once.

    Luke 12:37 Blessed are those slaves whom the master finds keeping watch when he comes. Amen, I tell you: he will gird himself, have them recline at table, and come and serve them.

    Pinned target record

  2. Song of Songs 5:2–5:4 shared image

    Both describe a beloved voice and knocking at a door with an appeal to open: “he knocks upon the door! Open to me.” In the Song the speaker hesitates to open; Revelation leaves the response to “anyone.”

    Read matching passage

    Song of Songs 5:2 I sleep, but my heart keeps watch. The voice of my kinsman—he knocks upon the door! Open to me, my sister, my companion, my dove, my perfect one, for my head is filled with dew and my curls with the drops of night.

    Song of Songs 5:3 I took off my tunic; how could I put it on? I washed my feet; how could I soil them?

    Song of Songs 5:4 My kinsman extended his hand from the opening, and my belly was stirred for him.

    Pinned target record

  3. John 14:21–14:23 parallel promise

    Both promise that the Lord will come to the one who responds and be with him: “I will come in unto him and dine with him” and “We will come to him and make Our dwelling with him.”

    Read matching passage

    John 14:21 The one who has My commandments and keeps them is the one who loves Me. And the one who loves Me will be loved by My Father, and I will love him and reveal Myself to him.”

    John 14:22 Judas—not the Iscariot—says to Him, “Lord, what then has happened that Thou art going to reveal Thyself to us and not to the world?”

    John 14:23 Jesus answered and said to him, “If anyone loves Me, he will keep My word, and My Father will love him, and We will come to him and make Our dwelling with him.

  1. Matthew 19:28 parallel promise

    Both promise that the faithful will sit on thrones alongside the Son: Matthew’s followers will “sit upon twelve thrones” when the Son of Man sits on “the throne of His glory.” Matthew specifies judging the twelve tribes; Revelation speaks of sharing Christ’s throne.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 20:4 shows faithful witnesses seated on “thrones” who “reigned with Christ,” corresponding to the promise that the conqueror will sit with Christ on his throne.

    Revelation 20:4 And I saw thrones, and they sat upon them, and judgment was given to them; and the souls of those beheaded because of the testimony of Jesus and because of the word of God, and those who did not worship the beast or its image and did not receive the mark upon their forehead and upon their hand. And they lived and reigned with Christ for a thousand years.

    Pinned target record

  3. John 16:33 shared theme of Christ’s victory

    Both ground the believer’s hope in Christ’s own victory: “as I also conquered” and “I have overcome the world.” The English wording differs (“conquered” / “overcome”).

    Read matching passage

    John 16:33 These things I have spoken to you so that in Me you may have peace. In the world you have affliction; but take courage: I have overcome the world.”

Revelation 4:3

  1. Ezekiel 1:28 shared image

    Both surround the divine presence with a bow-like radiance: Ezekiel sees “the appearance of a bow when it is in the cloud” in the brightness all around “the likeness of the Lord’s glory,” and John sees “a rainbow around the throne.” Ezekiel does not compare it to an emerald.

    Read matching passage

    Ezekiel 1:28 Like the appearance of a bow when it is in the cloud on a rainy day, so was the arrangement of the brightness all around. This was the appearance of the likeness of the Lord’s glory. I saw it, and I fall upon my face, and I heard the voice of One speaking.

    Both describe divine radiance as like “a jasper stone”: the One on the throne here, and the city “having the glory of God” in Revelation 21:11.

    The jasper, sardius and emerald named here all appear among the foundation stones of the new Jerusalem, where they adorn the city rather than describe God’s appearance.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 21:19 The foundations of the wall of the city were adorned with every precious stone: the first foundation jasper, the second sapphire, the third chalcedony, the fourth emerald,

    Revelation 21:20 the fifth sardonyx, the sixth sardius, the seventh chrysolite, the eighth beryl, the ninth topaz, the tenth chrysoprase, the eleventh hyacinth, the twelfth amethyst.

  1. Revelation 1:8 same divine title

    Both name God as “Lord God” and “Almighty” and as “the One who is and who was and who is coming,” though the order of the tenses differs.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 11:17 saying, “We give Thee thanks, Lord God Almighty, the One who is and who was, because Thou hast taken Thy great power and reigned.

  2. Daniel 12:7 shared divine title

    Both describe God as the one who lives for ever: the one clothed in fine linen swears “by God who lives for ever,” and the living creatures honour “the One living for the ages of the ages.”

    Both describe glory and honour given “to the One sitting upon the throne,” and both use the phrase “the ages of the ages” (in 4:9 as a title of God, in 5:13 for the duration of the praise).     Revelation 5:14 And the four living creatures were saying, “Amen.” And the elders fell and worshipped.

Revelation 5:1

And I saw upon the right hand of the One sitting upon the throne a scroll written within and on the back, sealed with seven seals.

  1. Ezekiel 2:9–2:10 shared image

    Both describe a scroll held in a heavenly hand with writing on both sides: Ezekiel’s scroll had “writing on the back and the front,” John’s is “written within and on the back.” Ezekiel’s scroll is unrolled, whereas John’s is sealed.

    Read matching passage

    Ezekiel 2:9 I looked, and behold, a hand was stretched toward me, and in it was a scroll of a book;

    Ezekiel 2:10 and He unrolled it before me. It had writing on the back and the front, and upon it were written a lament, a song and woe.

  2. Isaiah 29:11 shared image

    Both describe a sealed book: Isaiah’s “sealed book,” which the reader “cannot read, for it is sealed,” and the scroll “sealed with seven seals.”

    Read matching passage

    Pinned target record

    Both speak of seven eyes ranging over all the earth: Zechariah’s “seven are the Lord’s eyes, looking over all the earth,” and the Lamb’s “seven eyes… sent into all the earth.”

    Read matching passage

Revelation 6:2

  1. Zechariah 6:3–6:8 shared image

    Both visions include white horses that go out: in Zechariah “the white ones were going out,” and John’s rider on a white horse “went out conquering.” In Zechariah the horses draw chariots and are identified as the four winds of heaven.

    Read matching passage

    Zechariah 6:3 the third chariot white horses, and the fourth chariot dappled, gray horses.

    Zechariah 6:4 I answered and said to the angel speaking within me, “What are these, lord?”

    Zechariah 6:6 The one with the black horses was going out toward the northern land; the white ones were going out behind them, and the dappled ones were going out toward the southern land,

    Zechariah 6:7 and the gray ones were going out and looking to go and travel around the earth.” He said, “Go and travel around the earth!” And they traveled around the earth.

    Zechariah 6:8 He cried out and said to me, “Look, those going out toward the northern land have brought My wrath to rest in the northern land.”

    Both visions include a mounted rider and a white horse. In Zechariah the white horses are among several colours behind a man on a flame-red horse.

  3. Revelation 19:11–19:12 shared image

    Both open with “behold, a white horse” and a rider associated with crowns and warfare. The texts do not by themselves establish that the two riders are the same; the rider of Revelation 19 is called Faithful and True.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 19:11 And I saw heaven opened, and behold, a white horse, and the One sitting upon it is called Faithful and True, and in righteousness He judgeth and wageth war.

Revelation 6:4

And another horse went out, fiery red, and the one sitting upon it was given to take peace from the earth, so that they would slaughter one another; and a great sword was given to him.

    Both visions feature a rider on a red horse: Zechariah sees “a man mounted on a flame-red horse.” Zechariah’s rider is not given a sword or the power to take peace.

    Read matching passage

  3. Matthew 10:34 verbal echo

    Both pair the removal of peace on the earth with a sword: Jesus did not come “to bring peace upon the earth… but a sword,” and the rider is given “to take peace from the earth” and “a great sword.” Jesus speaks of the purpose of his coming, not of mutual slaughter.

    Read matching passage

    Matthew 10:34 Do not suppose that I came to bring peace upon the earth. I did not come to bring peace, but a sword.

Revelation 6:5

And when He opened the third seal, I heard the third living creature saying, “Come.” And I saw, and behold, a black horse, and the one sitting upon it had a balance in his hand.

    Both use the weighing of food as a sign of scarcity: Jerusalem will “eat bread by weight and in need,” and the rider holds “a balance in his hand.”

    Read matching passage

    Ezekiel 4:16 He said to me, “Son of man, behold, I am breaking the support of bread in Jerusalem. They will eat bread by weight and in need, and drink water by measure and in devastation,

    Pinned target record

  2. Leviticus 26:26 shared image

    Both use rationing by weight as a sign of judgement: God threatens that bread will be given back “by weight” and they will not be filled. Revelation shows the balance without stating the threat.

    Read matching passage

    Leviticus 26:26 When I afflict you with a scarcity of bread, ten women shall bake your bread in one oven and give back your bread by weight. You shall eat and in no wise be filled.

    Pinned target record

  3. Zechariah 6:2 shared image
