Revelation 6:6

And I heard something like a voice in the midst of the four living creatures saying, “A choenix of wheat for a denarius, and three choenices of barley for a denarius; and do not harm the oil and the wine.”

  1. Revelation 9:4 parallel restriction

    Both contain a command limiting the scope of a judgement by telling agents “not to harm” certain produce or vegetation: here “the oil and the wine,” in Revelation 9:4 “the grass of the earth or any green thing or any tree.” In Revelation 9:4 people without God’s seal are then targeted.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 9:4 And they were told not to harm the grass of the earth or any green thing or any tree, but only the people who do not have the seal of God upon their foreheads.

    Pinned target record

Revelation 6:7

And when He opened the fourth seal, I heard the voice of the fourth living creature saying, “Come.”

  1. Revelation 6:3 parallel seal formula

    Both verses use the same pattern for the seal openings: when He opened a numbered seal, the matching numbered living creature is heard saying “Come.” Revelation 6:3 gives the second instance and 6:7 the fourth.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 6:3 And when He opened the second seal, I heard the second living creature saying, “Come.”

    Pinned target record

  2. Revelation 4:7 internal cross-reference

    Revelation 4:7 introduces the “fourth living creature” who speaks at the fourth seal, describing it as “like an eagle flying.” The seal vision itself does not repeat this description.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 4:7 And the first living creature was like a lion, and the second living creature like a calf, and the third living creature had a face like a human being’s, and the fourth living creature was like an eagle flying.

    Pinned target record

Revelation 6:8

And I saw, and behold, a pale green horse, and the one sitting upon it had the name Death, and Hades followed with him. And authority was given to them over a fourth of the earth, to kill with sword and with famine and with death and by the beasts of the earth.

  1. Ezekiel 5:12 verbal allusion

    Ezekiel divides the judgment by quarters, with portions consumed “by death,” “by famine” and falling “by the sword.” Revelation gives authority over “a fourth of the earth” to kill with sword, famine and death; Ezekiel's fourth portion is scattering rather than beasts.

    Read matching passage

    Ezekiel 5:12 A quarter of you will be consumed by death; a quarter of you will perish by famine within you; a quarter of you I will scatter to every wind; and a quarter of you will fall by the sword around you, and I will draw a sword after them.

    Pinned target record

  2. Ezekiel 5:15–5:17 shared list of judgments

    Ezekiel 5:17 names famine, “harmful wild animals,” death and the sword as judgments sent by the Lord. Revelation's rider kills “with sword and with famine and with death and by the beasts of the earth,” the same four agents.

    Read matching passage

    Ezekiel 5:15 you will be mourned and ravaged among the nations around you when I execute judgments against you in the vengeance of My wrath. I, the Lord, have spoken.

    Ezekiel 5:16 When I send My arrows of famine against them, they will bring exhaustion, and I will break your support of bread.

    Ezekiel 5:17 I will send famine and harmful wild animals against you, and I will punish you. Death and blood will pass over you, and I will bring a sword upon you all around. I, the Lord, have spoken.’”

    Pinned target record

  3. Revelation 20:13–20:14 same personified figures

    Both passages treat Death and Hades as a personified pair: here Death rides and “Hades followed with him,” while in Revelation 20:13–14 “Death and Hades” give up their dead and are thrown into the lake of fire.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 20:13 And the sea gave the dead in it, and Death and Hades gave the dead in them, and they were judged, each according to their works.

    Revelation 20:14 And Death and Hades were thrown into the lake of fire. This is the second death, the lake of fire.

    Pinned target record

Revelation 6:9

And when He opened the fifth seal, I saw beneath the altar the souls of those slaughtered because of the word of God and because of the testimony they held.

  1. Revelation 20:4 parallel vision

    Both visions show “the souls” of those killed “because of the word of God” and because of testimony. Revelation 20:4 specifies they were beheaded for “the testimony of Jesus” and adds that they reign with Christ, whereas 6:9 places them “beneath the altar.”

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 20:4 And I saw thrones, and they sat upon them, and judgment was given to them; and the souls of those beheaded because of the testimony of Jesus and because of the word of God, and those who did not worship the beast or its image and did not receive the mark upon their forehead and upon their hand. And they lived and reigned with Christ for a thousand years.

    Pinned target record

  2. Revelation 1:9 shared phrase

    John is on Patmos “because of the word of God and the testimony of Jesus,” the same twofold cause given for the slaughter of the souls under the altar. John's case involves exile rather than death.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 1:9 I, John, your brother and fellow partner in the affliction and kingdom and endurance in Jesus, came to be on the island called Patmos because of the word of God and the testimony of Jesus.

    Pinned target record

Revelation 6:10

And they cried with a loud voice, saying, “How long, O Master, holy and true, before Thou dost judge and avenge our blood upon those dwelling on the earth?”

  1. Revelation 19:2 fulfilment of plea

    The martyrs ask when God will “judge and avenge our blood”; Revelation 19:2 declares that He “judged the great prostitute” and “avenged the blood of His slaves.” The martyrs address the Master as “holy and true,” and 19:2 calls His judgments “true and righteous.”

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 19:2 because His judgments are true and righteous, because He judged the great prostitute who corrupted the earth by her sexual immorality, and avenged the blood of His slaves from her hand.”

    Pinned target record

  2. Psalms 78:10 verbal allusion

    The psalm asks that “vengeance for the poured-out blood of Thy servants be known among the nations.” The martyrs' cry likewise seeks vengeance for their blood, though it is addressed directly to the “Master, holy and true.”

    Read matching passage

    Psalms 78:10 lest the nations say, “Where is their God?” And let vengeance for the poured-out blood of Thy servants be known among the nations before our eyes.

    Pinned target record

  3. Zechariah 1:12 shared lament form

    Both appeal to God with the question “how long,” using a sovereign title (“Lord Almighty”; “O Master”). Zechariah’s angel asks about mercy for Jerusalem and Judah’s cities, whereas the martyrs ask for judgment and vengeance.

    Read matching passage

    Zechariah 1:12 The angel of the Lord answered, “Lord Almighty, how long wilt Thou refuse mercy to Jerusalem and Judah’s cities, which Thou hast overlooked this seventieth year?”

    Pinned target record

Revelation 6:11

And a white robe was given to each of them, and they were told to rest for a little time yet, until their fellow slaves and their brothers, who were about to be killed as they themselves had been, were also complete in number.

  1. Revelation 7:9 shared image

    The white robe given to each martyr reappears in the great multitude “clothed in white robes” before the throne in Revelation 7:9. The later vision does not state that this multitude consists only of martyrs.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 7:9 After these things I saw, and behold, a great multitude that no one could number, from every nation and tribes and peoples and tongues, standing before the throne and before the Lamb, clothed in white robes, and palm branches in their hands.

    Pinned target record

  2. Daniel 12:13 verbal parallel

    Daniel is told to “go and rest,” for there are still days “until completion is fulfilled.” The martyrs are likewise told to rest “for a little time yet” until the number of their fellow slaves is complete.

    Read matching passage

    Daniel 12:13 But you, go and rest, for there are still days and hours until completion is fulfilled. You will rest and arise unto your glory at the completion of days.”

    Pinned target record

  3. Revelation 14:13 shared theme of rest

    Revelation 14:13 says the dead who die in the Lord “rest from their labours.” This connects with the instruction that the slain souls should “rest for a little time,” though 14:13 does not mention a waiting period.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 14:13 And I heard a voice from heaven saying, “Write: Blessed are the dead who die in the Lord from now on.” “Yea,” saith the Spirit, “so that they may rest from their labours, for their works follow with them.”

    Pinned target record

Revelation 6:12

And I saw when He opened the sixth seal, and a great earthquake occurred, and the sun became black like a haircloth sack, and the whole moon became like blood,

  1. Isaiah 50:3 shared image

    Isaiah says God will “clothe heaven in darkness and make its covering like sackcloth.” Revelation describes the sun becoming “black like a haircloth sack.” Isaiah speaks of heaven generally, not the sun in particular.

    Read matching passage

    Isaiah 50:3 I will clothe heaven in darkness and make its covering like sackcloth.”

    Pinned target record

  2. Joel 2:10 shared cosmic signs

    Joel describes the earth in turmoil, heaven shaken, and “the sun and moon will grow dark.” The sixth seal joins an earthquake with a darkened sun and altered moon, though Joel does not say the moon becomes like blood.

    Read matching passage

    Joel 2:10 Before them the earth will be thrown into turmoil and heaven shaken; the sun and moon will grow dark, and the stars will sink their brightness.

    Pinned target record

  3. Matthew 24:29 parallel prophecy

    Jesus foretells that “the sun will be darkened, the moon will not give its brightness” and the heavens shaken. The sixth seal shows the same failure of sun and moon, adding the great earthquake and blood-like moon.

    Read matching passage

    Matthew 24:29 Immediately after the affliction of those days, the sun will be darkened, the moon will not give its brightness, the stars will fall from heaven, and the powers of the heavens will be shaken.

    Pinned target record

Revelation 6:13

and the stars of heaven fell to the earth, as a fig tree throweth off its unripe figs when shaken by a great wind.

  1. Isaiah 34:4 verbal allusion

    Isaiah 34:4 pictures the stars falling “like leaves falling from a fig tree.” Revelation likewise compares falling stars to a fig tree shedding its figs; Isaiah speaks of leaves, Revelation of unripe figs shaken by wind.

    Read matching passage

    Isaiah 34:4 Heaven will roll up like a scroll, and all the stars will fall like leaves from a vine, like leaves falling from a fig tree.

    Pinned target record

  2. Matthew 24:29 parallel prophecy

    Jesus foretells that “the stars will fall from heaven,” the same event shown here as “the stars of heaven fell to the earth.” Matthew does not use the fig-tree comparison.

    Read matching passage

    Matthew 24:29 Immediately after the affliction of those days, the sun will be darkened, the moon will not give its brightness, the stars will fall from heaven, and the powers of the heavens will be shaken.

    Pinned target record

Revelation 6:14

And heaven withdrew like a scroll being rolled up, and every mountain and island were moved from their places.

  1. Isaiah 34:4 verbal allusion

    Isaiah says “heaven will roll up like a scroll,” and Revelation says heaven withdrew “like a scroll being rolled up.” The image and comparison are the same in both texts.

    Read matching passage

    Isaiah 34:4 Heaven will roll up like a scroll, and all the stars will fall like leaves from a vine, like leaves falling from a fig tree.

    Pinned target record

  2. Revelation 16:20 parallel judgment

    At the seventh bowl “every island fled, and mountains were not found,” echoing the sixth seal's “every mountain and island were moved from their places.” The bowl text speaks of disappearance rather than displacement.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 16:20 And every island fled, and mountains were not found.

    Pinned target record

  3. Revelation 20:11 parallel vision

    Before the great white throne “the earth and heaven fled, and no place was found for them,” comparable to heaven withdrawing and mountains and islands moved from their places. Revelation 20:11 is set at the final judgment.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 20:11 And I saw a great white throne and the One sitting upon it, from whose face the earth and heaven fled, and no place was found for them.

Revelation 6:15

And the kings of the earth and the great ones and the commanders of thousands and the rich and the strong and every slave and free person hid themselves in the caves and among the rocks of the mountains.

  1. Isaiah 2:19 verbal allusion

    Isaiah speaks of carrying things “into caves, into clefts in the rocks” from the Lord’s terror when He rises to shatter the earth. Revelation shows the kings and people of every rank hiding themselves “in the caves and among the rocks of the mountains.”

    Read matching passage

    Isaiah 2:19 carrying them into caves, into clefts in the rocks and into holes in the earth, from the Lord’s terror and the glory of His strength, whenever He rises to shatter the earth.

    Pinned target record

  2. Isaiah 2:10 verbal allusion

    Isaiah 2:10 commands “enter the rocks and hide in the earth from the Lord's terror.” Revelation describes people of every rank who “hid themselves” among the rocks; Isaiah's verse is an imperative, Revelation's a narrative.

    Read matching passage

    Isaiah 2:10 And now enter the rocks and hide in the earth from the Lord’s terror and the glory of His strength, whenever He rises to shatter the earth.

Revelation 6:16

And they say to the mountains and the rocks, “Fall upon us and hide us from the face of the One sitting upon the throne and from the wrath of the Lamb,

  1. Hosea 10:8 verbal allusion

    Hosea has the people say to the mountains “Cover us!” and to the hills “Fall upon us!” Revelation adapts the cry as “Fall upon us and hide us,” addressed to the mountains and rocks, adding hiding from the face of the One on the throne and from the Lamb’s wrath.

    Read matching passage

    Hosea 10:8 The shrines of On, Israel’s sins, will be removed; thorns and thistles will grow on their altars. They will say to the mountains, ‘Cover us!’ and to the hills, ‘Fall upon us!’

    Pinned target record

  2. Luke 23:30 verbal parallel

    Jesus says people will begin “to say to the mountains, ‘Fall upon us,’ and to the hills, ‘Cover us.’” Revelation uses the same cry to the mountains, “Fall upon us.”

    Read matching passage

    The people seek to hide “from the face of the One sitting upon the throne,” and Revelation 20:11 describes the One sitting on the throne “from whose face the earth and heaven fled.” Both stress flight from His face.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 20:11 And I saw a great white throne and the One sitting upon it, from whose face the earth and heaven fled, and no place was found for them.

    Malachi asks “who will stand at his appearance?” concerning the day of the Lord's coming. Revelation closes with the same question, “who is able to stand?” on the great day of wrath.

    Zephaniah calls the Lord's “great day” a “day of wrath,” the combination used in “the great day of Their wrath.” Zephaniah's day falls on the earth's inhabitants; Revelation attributes the wrath to the One on the throne and the Lamb.

    Both verses give a number of “myriads of myriads”. In Revelation 5:11 they are angels around the throne; here they are mounted armies.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 5:11 And I saw, and heard a voice of many angels around the throne and the living creatures and the elders; and their number was myriads of myriads and thousands of thousands,

    Pinned target record

  2. Daniel 7:10 shared image

    Daniel 7:10 counts “ten thousand times ten thousand” standing before the Ancient One, a figure close to Revelation’s “two myriads of myriads”. Daniel’s host attends a heavenly court, not a cavalry army.

    Read matching passage

    Daniel 7:10 and a river of fire came out before Him. A thousand thousands attended Him, and ten thousand times ten thousand stood beside Him. A court sat, and books were opened.

    Pinned target record

  3. Revelation 7:4 verbal parallel

    In both verses the seer reports a number he heard: “I heard the number” in 7:4 and “I heard their number” in 9:16. Revelation 7:4 gives the number of those sealed; 9:16 the number of the cavalry.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 7:4 And I heard the number of those sealed: a hundred forty-four thousand, sealed from every tribe of the sons of Israel.

    Pinned target record

Revelation 9:17

And thus I saw the horses in the vision and those sitting upon them, having breastplates of fiery red and hyacinth blue and sulfur yellow; and the heads of the horses were like lions’ heads, and from their mouths come out fire and smoke and sulfur.

  1. Revelation 9:18 internal cross-reference

    Revelation 9:18 repeats “fire and smoke and sulfur” coming out of the horses’ mouths and calls them three plagues that killed a third of the people.

    Both the locusts of the fifth trumpet and the cavalry of the sixth are described as wearing “breastplates”. The locusts’ breastplates are “like iron breastplates”; the riders’ are fiery red, hyacinth blue and sulfur yellow.

Revelation 9:19

For the authority of the horses is in their mouths and in their tails, for their tails are like snakes, having heads, and with them they harm.

  1. Revelation 9:10 verbal parallel

    Both verses place the creatures’ “authority” “in their tails” and say they “harm” with them. Revelation 9:10 concerns the scorpion-tailed locusts; 9:19 the horses whose tails are like snakes with heads.

Revelation 9:20

And the rest of the people, who were not killed by these plagues, did not even repent of the works of their hands, so as not to worship the demons and the idols of gold and silver and bronze and stone and wood, which can neither see nor hear nor walk.

  1. Revelation 9:21 internal cross-reference

    Revelation 9:21 continues the same sentence of judgment: those who survived the plagues “did not repent” of their murders, sorceries, sexual immorality or thefts, as well as of their idolatry.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 9:21 And they did not repent of their murders or their sorcerous potions or their sexual immorality or their thefts.

    Pinned target record

  2. Jeremiah 1:16 verbal allusion

    Jeremiah 1:16 condemns those who “bowed down to the works of their hands”. Revelation 9:20 uses the same phrase, “the works of their hands”, for the idols that the survivors kept worshipping.

    Read matching passage

    Jeremiah 1:16 I will speak to them with judgment concerning all their evil, because they abandoned Me, sacrificed to foreign gods and bowed down to the works of their hands.

    Pinned target record

  3. Daniel 5:23 shared theme

    Daniel 5:23 rebukes a king who “praised all the idols made by human hands” but did not bless the living God. The Daniel text shown does not list the idols’ materials or say they cannot see or hear, as Revelation 9:20 does.

    Read matching passage

    Daniel 5:23 O king, you held a banquet for your friends and drank wine. The vessels of the living God’s house were brought to you, and you and your magnates drank from them and praised all the idols made by human hands. But you did not bless the living God. Your breath is in His hand, and He gave you your kingdom, yet you neither blessed Him nor offered Him praise.

    Pinned target record

Revelation 9:21

And they did not repent of their murders or their sorcerous potions or their sexual immorality or their thefts.

  1. Revelation 21:8 shared vice list

    Revelation 21:8 names “murderers”, the “sexually immoral” and “sorcerers” among those whose share is in the lake of fire. These are three of the four sins of which the survivors in 9:21 do not repent.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 21:8 But as for the cowardly and unbelieving and abominable and murderers and sexually immoral and sorcerers and idolaters and all liars, their share is in the lake burning with fire and sulfur, which is the second death.”

    Pinned target record

  2. Revelation 22:15 shared vice list

    Revelation 22:15 places “the sorcerers and the sexually immoral and the murderers” outside the city. These match three of the four sins named in 9:21.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 22:15 Outside are the dogs and the sorcerers and the sexually immoral and the murderers and the idolaters and everyone loving and practicing falsehood.

  3. Matthew 15:19 shared vice list

    Matthew 15:19 lists “murders”, “sexual immoralities” and “thefts” among the evils that come out of the heart. Three of the four sins in Revelation 9:21 appear, but sorcery does not.

    Read matching passage

    Matthew 15:19 For out of the heart come evil reasonings, murders, adulteries, sexual immoralities, thefts, false testimonies, and blasphemies.

  3. Revelation 1:15–1:16 parallel vision

    Both descriptions give the figure a face or appearance “like the sun” and describe his feet: “like burnished bronze” in 1:15 and “like pillars of fire” in 10:1. In chapter 1 the figure is the risen Christ; in chapter 10 he is called an angel.

    Daniel is told to “seal the book until the time of completion”, and the seer is told to “Seal up” what the seven thunders spoke. In Revelation the seer is also forbidden to write the words at all.

    Revelation 22:10 reverses the command of 10:4: “Do not seal the words of the prophecy of this scroll, for the time is near.” The thunders’ words stay sealed, while the book of the prophecy itself is left open.

  1. Daniel 12:7 verbal allusion

    Daniel’s figure raises his hands “to heaven” and swears by God who lives for ever; Revelation’s angel, standing upon the sea and upon the earth, raises his right hand “to heaven”. Daniel’s figure raises both right and left hands.

    Read matching passage

    Daniel 12:7 I heard the one clothed in fine linen above the river’s water say, “Until the time of completion.” He raised his right hand and his left to heaven and swore by God who lives for ever that in a time, times, and half a time would be the completion of the “hands of release” of a holy people, and all these things would be completed.

    Pinned target record

  2. Deuteronomy 32:40 verbal allusion

    In Deuteronomy the Lord says, “I will lift My hand unto heaven, and swear”, together with “I live for ever”. Revelation 10:5–6 combines the same raised hand with an oath by the One who lives for ever.

Revelation 10:9

And I went to the angel, telling him to give me the little scroll. And he saith unto me, “Take and devour it. It shall make thy belly bitter, but in thy mouth it shall be sweet as honey.”

  1. Ezekiel 3:1–3:3 verbal allusion

    Ezekiel is told to “eat this scroll”, his belly is to be filled with it, and in his mouth it is “like sweet honey”. Revelation 10:9 takes up the scroll, the eating, the belly and sweetness “as honey” in the mouth, and adds that it will make the belly bitter.

    Read matching passage

    Ezekiel 3:1 He said to me, “Son of man, eat this scroll, then go and speak to the sons of Israel.”

    Ezekiel 3:2 I opened my mouth wide, and He fed me the scroll.

    Ezekiel 3:3 He said to me, “Son of man, your mouth will eat, and your belly will be filled with this scroll that has been given to you.” I ate it, and in my mouth it became like sweet honey.

  2. Ezekiel 2:8 verbal allusion

    Ezekiel 2:8 tells the prophet to “Open your mouth wide and eat what I give you”. In Revelation 10:9 the angel tells the seer, of the little scroll, “Take and devour it”.

    Read matching passage

    Ezekiel 2:8 And you, son of man, hear the One speaking to you. Do not become embittering like the embittering house. Open your mouth wide and eat what I give you.”

Revelation 10:10

And I took the little scroll from the angel’s hand and devoured it, and in my mouth it was sweet as honey; and when I ate it, my belly was made bitter.

  1. Ezekiel 3:3 verbal allusion

    Both prophets eat a scroll given to them, and in Ezekiel’s words it was “in my mouth” “like sweet honey”, as in Revelation’s “in my mouth it was sweet as honey”. Ezekiel 3:3 mentions the belly being filled but not made bitter.

    Read matching passage

    Ezekiel 3:3 He said to me, “Son of man, your mouth will eat, and your belly will be filled with this scroll that has been given to you.” I ate it, and in my mouth it became like sweet honey.

Revelation 11:1

And a reed like a rod was given to me, with the words, “Rise and measure the temple of God and the altar and those worshipping in it.

  1. Ezekiel 42:15–42:20 shared image

    Ezekiel’s guide measures the house all around “with the measuring reed”, “to distinguish between the holy areas and the forewall”. Revelation 11:1 gives the seer a reed to measure the temple of God, and 11:2 excludes the outer court.

    Read matching passage

    Ezekiel 42:15 The measurement of the house inside was completed. He brought me out by the gate facing east and measured the plan of the house all around in its arrangement.

    Ezekiel 42:16 He stood behind the gate facing east and measured five hundred with the measuring reed.

    Ezekiel 42:17 He turned toward the north and measured the north-facing side: five hundred cubits with the measuring reed.

    Ezekiel 42:18 He turned toward the sea and measured the sea-facing side: five hundred with the measuring reed.

    Ezekiel 42:19 He turned toward the south and measured opposite the south: five hundred with the measuring reed.

    Ezekiel 42:20 The four sides were measured with the same reed. He laid it out, with their enclosure all around: five hundred toward the east and five hundred cubits in breadth, to distinguish between the holy areas and the forewall in the arrangement of the house.

    Pinned target record

  2. Revelation 21:15 internal cross-reference

    Revelation 21:15 has a “golden measuring reed” used to measure the city, its gates and its wall. In 11:1 the seer himself is given a reed to measure the temple, altar and worshippers.

    Read matching passage

Revelation 11:2

And leave out the court outside the temple, and do not measure it, because it was given to the nations, and they will trample the holy city for forty-two months.

  1. Luke 21:24 verbal allusion

    Luke 21:24 says “Jerusalem will be trampled by nations” until the times of nations are fulfilled. Revelation 11:2 says the nations “will trample the holy city” for forty-two months. Revelation does not name the city here.

    Read matching passage

    Luke 21:24 They will fall by the edge of the sword and be led captive into all the nations. And Jerusalem will be trampled by nations until the times of nations are fulfilled.

    Pinned target record

  2. Revelation 11:3 internal cross-reference

    Revelation 11:3 sets the witnesses’ prophesying at “a thousand two hundred sixty days”. This is a period stated in days next to the “forty-two months” of the trampling in 11:2.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 11:3 And I will grant authority to My two witnesses, and they will prophesy for a thousand two hundred sixty days, clothed in sackcloth.”

    Pinned target record

  3. Daniel 7:25 shared theme

    In Daniel 7:25 the one who will “wear down the holy ones of the Most High” has everything handed into his hands “for a time, times, and half a time”. Revelation 11:2 gives a set period, forty-two months, for the nations to trample the holy city. Neither text states that the two periods are equal.

    Read matching passage

    Daniel 7:25 He will speak words against the Most High, wear down the holy ones of the Most High, and expect to alter seasons and law. Everything will be handed into his hands for a time, times, and half a time.

    Pinned target record

Revelation 11:3

And I will grant authority to My two witnesses, and they will prophesy for a thousand two hundred sixty days, clothed in sackcloth.”

  1. Revelation 12:6 internal cross-reference

    Revelation 12:6 uses the same period, “a thousand two hundred sixty days”, for the time the woman is nourished in the wilderness.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 12:6 And the woman fled into the wilderness, where she hath a place prepared by God, so that there they may nourish her for a thousand two hundred sixty days.

    Pinned target record

  2. Deuteronomy 19:15 background

    Deuteronomy 19:15 requires testimony “At the mouth of two witnesses”. Revelation 11:3 sends exactly “two witnesses”, though Revelation does not cite the legal rule.

    Read matching passage

    Deuteronomy 19:15 One witness shall not suffice to testify against a man concerning any injustice, or any trespass, or any sin which he sins. At the mouth of two witnesses, and at the mouth of three witnesses, shall every matter be established.

    Pinned target record

  3. Revelation 11:2 internal cross-reference

    Revelation 11:2 gives the nations “forty-two months” to trample the holy city, a period stated in months next to the 1,260 days of the witnesses’ prophesying.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 11:2 And leave out the court outside the temple, and do not measure it, because it was given to the nations, and they will trample the holy city for forty-two months.

    Pinned target record

Revelation 11:4

These are the two olive trees and the two lampstands standing before the Lord of the earth.

  1. Zechariah 4:11–4:14 verbal allusion

    Zechariah asks about “these two olive trees” and is told that they “stand beside the Lord of all the earth”. Revelation 11:4 calls the witnesses “the two olive trees” “standing before the Lord of the earth”.

    Read matching passage

    Zechariah 4:11 I answered him, “What are these two olive trees to the lampstand’s right and left?”

    Zechariah 4:12 I asked a second time, “What are the two olive branches in the hands of the two golden nozzles that pour upon and draw up the golden pouring channels?”

    Zechariah 4:13 He said to me, “Do you not know what these are?” I said, “No, lord.”

    Zechariah 4:14 He said, “These two sons of richness stand beside the Lord of all the earth.”

    Pinned target record

  2. Zechariah 4:2–4:3 shared image

    Zechariah’s vision sets “Two olive trees” beside a golden lampstand. Revelation pairs “two olive trees” with “two lampstands”, where Zechariah has a single lampstand.

    Read matching passage

    Zechariah 4:2 and said to me, “What do you see?” I said, “I see a lampstand entirely of gold, with its lamp-bowl above it, seven lamps upon it, and seven pouring channels for the lamps above it.

    Zechariah 4:3 Two olive trees stand over it, one to the right of the lamp-bowl and one to the left.”

    Pinned target record

  3. Revelation 1:20 internal cross-reference

    Revelation 1:20 explains the lampstands as “seven Churches”. This gives the book’s own key to lampstand imagery, although 11:4 has two lampstands, not seven.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 1:20 The mystery of the seven stars thou sawest upon My right hand, and the seven golden lampstands: the seven stars are angels of the seven Churches, and the seven lampstands are seven Churches.”

Revelation 11:5

And if anyone wisheth to harm them, fire cometh out of their mouth and devoureth their enemies; and if anyone should wish to harm them, in this way he must be killed.

  1. Jeremiah 5:14 verbal allusion

    The Lord makes His words “fire in your mouth” to “devour” the people, and the witnesses have fire that “cometh out of their mouth and devoureth their enemies”.

    Read matching passage

    Jeremiah 5:14 Therefore thus says the Lord Almighty: Because you spoke this utterance, behold, I have made My words fire in your mouth, and this people wood, and it will devour them.

    Pinned target record

  2. 2 Kings 1:10–1:12 shared image

    Elijah calls down fire that consumes the men sent against him, and the witnesses destroy those who would harm them with fire. Elijah’s fire comes “down from heaven”, while the witnesses’ fire comes out of their mouth.

    Read matching passage

    2 Kings 1:10 Elijah answered and said to the captain of fifty, ‘If I am a man of God, fire will come down from heaven and consume you and your fifty men.’ Fire came down from heaven and consumed him and his fifty men.

    2 Kings 1:11 The king again sent to him another captain of fifty with his fifty men. The captain went up and spoke to him, saying, ‘Man of God, thus says the king: Come down quickly!’

    2 Kings 1:12 Elijah answered and spoke to him, saying, ‘If I am a man of God, fire will come down from heaven and consume you and your fifty men.’ Fire came down from heaven and consumed him and his fifty men.

Revelation 11:6

These have authority to shut heaven, so that rain may not fall during the days of their prophecy; and they have authority over the waters to turn them into blood and to strike the earth with every plague as often as they wish.

  1. 1 Kings 17:1 shared image

    Elijah declares there will be “neither dew nor rain” except at his word. The witnesses likewise “have authority to shut heaven, so that rain may not fall” during their prophecy.

    Read matching passage

    1 Kings 17:1 Elijah the prophet, the Tishbite from Thesbon in Gilead, said to Ahab, “As the Lord, the God of hosts, the God of Israel, lives, before whom I stand, there will be neither dew nor rain these years except through the utterance of my word.”

    Pinned target record

  2. Luke 4:25 verbal parallel

    Luke 4:25 recalls Elijah’s days “when heaven was shut”, which matches Revelation’s “authority to shut heaven”. Luke gives the drought’s length as three years and six months.

    Read matching passage

    Luke 4:25 But in truth I tell you, there were many widows in Israel in Elijah’s days, when heaven was shut for three years and six months and a great famine came over the whole land.

    Pinned target record

  3. 1 Samuel 4:8 verbal allusion

    The Philistines recall the gods who “struck Egypt with every plague”. Revelation gives the witnesses power “to strike the earth with every plague”, alongside the Egyptian sign of turning waters into blood.

    Read matching passage

    1 Samuel 4:8 Woe to us! Who will rescue us from the hand of these powerful gods? These are the gods who struck Egypt with every plague and in the wilderness.

    Read matching passage

    Psalms 78:2 They made the dead bodies of Thy servants food for the birds of heaven, the flesh of Thy holy ones for the beasts of the earth.

    Psalms 78:3 They poured out their blood like water around Jerusalem, and there was no one to bury them.

Revelation 11:10

  1. Revelation 11:5–11:6 internal cross-reference

    Revelation 11:5–6 describes the fire, drought and plagues by which the two prophets “tormented those dwelling upon the earth”, the reason for the celebration in 11:10.

    Micah tells the enemy, “Do not rejoice over me … because I have fallen! I will rise”. Revelation pictures enemies rejoicing over the fallen prophets, who rise again in 11:11.

    Read matching passage

Revelation 11:13

And in that hour a great earthquake occurred, and a tenth of the city fell, and seven thousand human names were killed in the earthquake; and the rest became fearful and gave glory to the God of heaven.

  1. Revelation 14:7 verbal parallel

    Revelation 14:7 calls people to “Fear God and give Him glory”. This is the response recorded of the survivors in 11:13, who “became fearful and gave glory to the God of heaven”.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 14:7 saying in a loud voice, “Fear God and give Him glory, because the hour of His judgment hath come, and worship the One who made heaven and the earth and sea and springs of waters.”

    Pinned target record

  2. Revelation 16:9–16:11 contrast

    In Revelation 16:9–11 people “did not repent to give Him glory” and blasphemed “the God of heaven”. In 11:13 the survivors do give glory to the God of heaven.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 16:9 And people were scorched with great heat, and blasphemed the name of God, who hath authority over these plagues, and did not repent to give Him glory.

    Revelation 16:10 And the fifth poured out his bowl upon the beast’s throne, and its kingdom became darkened, and they were chewing their tongues because of the pain,

    Revelation 16:11 and blasphemed the God of heaven because of their pains and their sores, and did not repent of their works.

    Pinned target record

  3. Revelation 16:18–16:19 internal cross-reference

    Both passages report “a great earthquake” that brings down part of a city. In 11:13 a tenth of the city falls; in 16:18–19 the great city splits into three parts.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 16:18 And there were lightnings and voices and thunders, and a great earthquake occurred, such as had not occurred since human beings came to be upon the earth—so mighty an earthquake, so great.

    Revelation 16:19 And the great city became three parts, and the cities of the nations fell. And Babylon the great was remembered before God, to give her the cup of the wine of the fury of His wrath.

Revelation 12:2

  1. Isaiah 66:7–66:9 shared image

    Isaiah speaks of a woman in labor who “bore a male” and of Zion who “labored and bore her children”; Revelation’s woman likewise “crieth out in labour and torment to give birth.” Isaiah stresses a birth before the pains, while Revelation dwells on the torment.

    Read matching passage

    Isaiah 66:7 Before the woman in labor gave birth, before the pain of labor came, she escaped and bore a male.

    Isaiah 66:8 Who heard such a thing, and who has seen anything like it? Did a land labor in one day, or was a nation born all at once? For Zion labored and bore her children.

    Isaiah 66:9 ‘But I gave this expectation, and you did not remember Me,’ said the Lord. ‘Behold, did I not make the woman who gives birth and the barren woman?’ said God.

    Pinned target record

  3. John 16:21 shared image

    Jesus speaks of a woman giving birth who has “sorrow” and “anguish” until the child is born. Revelation 12:2 describes her “labour and torment to give birth”.

    Read matching passage

    John 16:21 When a woman is giving birth, she has sorrow because her hour has come; but when she has borne the child, she no longer remembers the anguish because of the joy that a human being has been born into the world.

Revelation 12:4

And his tail draggeth a third of the stars of heaven and threw them to the earth. And the dragon standeth before the woman about to give birth, so that when she giveth birth he may devour her child.

  1. Revelation 12:2–12:3 internal cross-reference

    Revelation 12:2–3 introduces the woman in labour and the great fiery red dragon who stand facing each other in 12:4.

  2. Exodus 1:16 shared theme

    Pharaoh orders that Hebrew male children be killed as the women are “about to give birth”. The dragon waits before the woman “about to give birth” to devour her child.

    Read matching passage

    Daniel’s horn is “raised as high as heaven’s stars” and then dashed to the earth, much as the dragon’s tail throws “a third of the stars of heaven” to the earth. In the Daniel text shown it is the horn, not the stars, that is dashed to the earth.

    Read matching passage

    Daniel 8:9 From one of them a single strong horn sprouted. It grew strong and struck toward the south, toward the east, and toward the north.

    Daniel 8:10 It was raised as high as heaven’s stars, but was dashed to the earth from the stars and trampled by them,

    Daniel 8:11 until the chief commander rescues the captivity. Because of him the ancient mountains were dashed down; their place and sacrifice were removed. He set it down upon the earth, and it prospered and came to pass, and the sanctuary will be made desolate.

    Daniel 8:12 Sins came upon the sacrifice; righteousness was thrown down to the ground, and he acted and prospered.

  3. Mark 16:19 shared image

    Mark records that the Lord Jesus “was taken up into heaven and sat at the right of God”. Revelation’s child “was caught up to God and to His throne”. Revelation 12:5 does not name the child.

    Read matching passage

    Mark 16:19 So the Lord Jesus, after speaking to them, was taken up into heaven and sat at the right of God.

Revelation 12:6

And the woman fled into the wilderness, where she hath a place prepared by God, so that there they may nourish her for a thousand two hundred sixty days.

  1. Revelation 12:14 internal cross-reference

    Revelation 12:14 retells the woman’s flight “into the wilderness to her place, where she is nourished”. There the period is given as “a time and times and half a time” instead of 1,260 days.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 12:14 And the two wings of the great eagle were given to the woman, so that she might fly into the wilderness to her place, where she is nourished for a time and times and half a time, away from the face of the serpent.

    Pinned target record

  2. Revelation 11:2–11:3 internal cross-reference

    Revelation 11:3 uses the same “thousand two hundred sixty days” for the prophesying of the two witnesses, and 11:2 has forty-two months.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 11:2 And leave out the court outside the temple, and do not measure it, because it was given to the nations, and they will trample the holy city for forty-two months.

    Revelation 11:3 And I will grant authority to My two witnesses, and they will prophesy for a thousand two hundred sixty days, clothed in sackcloth.”

    Pinned target record

  3. 1 Kings 19:4–19:8 shared image

    Elijah goes “into the wilderness” and is fed there by the Lord’s angel. The woman flees into the wilderness to a place where she is nourished. Elijah’s flight is not tied to a set number of days, except the later forty days’ journey.

    Read matching passage

    1 Kings 19:4 He himself went a day’s journey into the wilderness, came, and sat under a single broom bush. He asked that his life might end and said, “Let it be enough now! I pray Thee, take my life from me, Lord, for I am no better than my fathers.”

    1 Kings 19:5 He lay down and slept there under a plant. Behold, someone touched him and said to him, “Rise and eat.”

    1 Kings 19:6 Elijah looked, and there at his head were a hearth cake of spelt and a flask of water. He rose, ate, and drank, then lay down again.

    1 Kings 19:7 The Lord’s angel returned a second time, touched him, and said to him, “Rise, eat, for the journey is too much for you.”

    1 Kings 19:8 He rose, ate, and drank, and went in the strength of that food forty days and forty nights to Mount Horeb.

Revelation 12:7

And war arose in heaven: Michael and his angels were to wage war with the dragon. And the dragon and his angels fought,

  1. Jude 1:9 same named figure

    Jude calls Michael “the archangel” and describes him “contending with the devil”. Revelation shows Michael and his angels waging war with the dragon, who is identified in 12:9 as the Devil. Jude’s dispute is over the body of Moses.

    Read matching passage

    Jude 1:9 But Michael the archangel, when he was contending with the devil and disputing about the body of Moses, did not dare to bring a slanderous judgment against him, but said, “May the Lord rebuke you.”

    Daniel 10:13 shows “Michael, one of the foremost rulers” coming to help against a hostile power. Revelation 12:7 likewise shows Michael in conflict, here against the dragon in heaven.

    Pinned target record

  3. Daniel 12:1 same named figure

    Daniel 12:1 calls Michael “the great angel who stands over the sons of your people” in a time of great affliction. This is the same angel who leads the heavenly war in Revelation 12:7.

    Read matching passage

    Daniel 12:1 “At that hour Michael will pass by, the great angel who stands over the sons of your people. That will be a day of affliction such as has not occurred since they came into being until that day. On that day all the people found inscribed in the book will be exalted.

and he did not prevail, nor was a place found for them any more in heaven.

And the great dragon was thrown down, the ancient serpent, who is called the Devil and Satan, the one deceiving the whole inhabited world; he was thrown to the earth, and his angels were thrown with him.

  1. Luke 10:18 shared image

    Jesus says, “I was watching Satan fall from heaven”. Revelation 12:9 shows Satan “thrown down” to the earth after the war in heaven.

  2. Genesis 3:1 background

    Genesis 3:1 introduces “the serpent” who deceives the woman. Revelation identifies the dragon as “the ancient serpent” and “the one deceiving the whole inhabited world”. Genesis does not itself name the serpent as the Devil.

    Read matching passage

    Genesis 3:1 Now the serpent was more cunning than all the wild beasts upon the earth that the Lord God had made. And the serpent said to the woman, “Why has God said, ‘You shall not eat from any tree in the garden’?”

  3. Revelation 20:10 internal cross-reference

    Revelation 20:10 completes the dragon’s fall: “the devil who deceived them” is thrown into the lake of fire. The verb “thrown” and the role of deceiver are the same as in 12:9.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 20:10 And the devil who deceived them was thrown into the lake of fire and sulfur, where also are the beast and the false prophet, and they will be tormented day and night for the ages of the ages.

Revelation 12:10

And I heard a loud voice in heaven saying, “Now have come the salvation and the power and the kingdom of our God and the authority of His Christ, because the accuser of our brothers hath been thrown down, the one accusing them before our God day and night.

  1. Revelation 11:15 parallel heavenly proclamation

    In both verses voices in heaven announce “the kingdom” of “our God”/“our Lord” together with “His Christ.” Revelation 11:15 speaks of the kingdom of the world becoming His, while 12:10 links the arrival of the kingdom to the accuser being thrown down.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 11:15 And the seventh angel sounded, and loud voices arose in heaven, saying, “The kingdom of the world hath become the kingdom of our Lord and of His Christ, and He will reign for the ages of the ages.”

    Zechariah sees “the slanderer” standing beside Joshua the high priest “to oppose him” before the angel of the Lord, as Revelation describes one “accusing them before our God.” Zechariah ends with the Lord’s rebuke of the slanderer rather than his being thrown down.

    Read matching passage

    Zechariah 3:1 He showed me Joshua the high priest standing before the angel of the Lord, with the slanderer standing at his right hand to oppose him.

    Zechariah 3:2 The Lord said to the slanderer, “May the Lord rebuke you, slanderer! May the Lord, who chose Jerusalem, rebuke you! Look, is this not like a brand pulled from fire?”

    Pinned target record

  3. Job 1:9 shared image of the accuser

    Job shows “the devil” speaking “before the Lord” to cast doubt on a righteous man’s devotion, matching the picture of an accuser of the faithful before God. Job does not describe the accuser being cast down.

    Read matching passage

    Job 1:9 The devil answered before the Lord, “Does Job worship God for nothing?

Revelation 12:11

And they conquered him because of the blood of the Lamb and because of the word of their testimony, and they did not love their life until death.

  1. Revelation 20:4 parallel martyr description

    Both describe faithful people who die for their witness: “the word of their testimony” and “did not love their life until death” in 12:11, those “beheaded because of the testimony of Jesus and because of the word of God” in 20:4. Revelation 20:4 adds that they lived and reigned with Christ.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 20:4 And I saw thrones, and they sat upon them, and judgment was given to them; and the souls of those beheaded because of the testimony of Jesus and because of the word of God, and those who did not worship the beast or its image and did not receive the mark upon their forehead and upon their hand. And they lived and reigned with Christ for a thousand years.

    Pinned target record

  2. Acts 20:24 verbal echo

    Paul says “I consider my life of no value to myself” for the sake of bearing “solemn witness,” close to the conquerors who “did not love their life until death” because of their testimony. Paul speaks of his own ministry, not of a conflict with the accuser.

    Read matching passage

    Acts 20:24 But I consider my life of no value to myself, so that I may complete my course and the ministry I received from the Lord Jesus: to bear solemn witness to the good news of God’s grace.

Revelation 12:12

For this reason rejoice, heavens and those dwelling in them. Woe to the earth and the sea, because the devil came down to you, having great fury, knowing that he hath a little time.”

  1. Revelation 8:13 parallel woe announcement

    Both pronounce “Woe” on the earth’s inhabitants in view of coming disaster: an eagle’s threefold woe because of the remaining trumpets, and in 12:12 because “the devil came down to you.”

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 8:13 And I saw, and heard one eagle flying in midheaven saying with a loud voice, “Woe, woe, woe to those dwelling upon the earth, because of the remaining voices of the trumpet of the three angels who are about to sound!”

    Pinned target record

  2. Isaiah 44:23 verbal echo

    Isaiah opens with the same summons, “Rejoice, heavens,” grounded in God’s saving act for Israel. Isaiah calls the earth to rejoice as well, whereas Revelation pronounces woe on the earth and the sea.

    Read matching passage

    Isaiah 44:23 Rejoice, heavens, because God had mercy on Israel! Sound trumpets, earth’s foundations! Cry out joy, mountains, hills and all their trees! For God redeemed Jacob, and Israel will be glorified.

    Pinned target record

  3. Isaiah 49:13 verbal echo

    Isaiah begins “Rejoice, heavens,” giving the reason that God had mercy on His people. Unlike Revelation, Isaiah calls the earth to exult rather than announcing woe upon it.

    Read matching passage

    Isaiah 49:13 Rejoice, heavens, and let earth exult! Let mountains burst out in joy and hills in righteousness, for God had mercy on His people and comforted His people’s lowly ones.

    Pinned target record

  1. Revelation 12:3–12:5 internal cross-reference

    Revelation 12:3–5 introduces the dragon standing before the woman to devour her child and records that “she bore a son, a male.” Verse 13 resumes that scene: the dragon now pursues “the woman who bore the male child.”

    Genesis announces enmity between the one addressed and “the woman,” and between their seeds, as Revelation shows hostility directed at “the woman” and her child. The Genesis verse as shown does not name the addressee; Revelation calls the dragon a serpent elsewhere in the chapter.

    Read matching passage

    Genesis 3:15 And I will put enmity between you and the woman, and between your seed and her seed. He shall watch for your head, and you shall watch for his heel.

Revelation 12:14

  1. Revelation 12:6 internal cross-reference

    Revelation 12:6 already reports the woman fleeing “into the wilderness” to a “place” where she is nourished. Verse 14 repeats the scene, giving the period as “a time and times and half a time” rather than “a thousand two hundred sixty days.”

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 12:6 And the woman fled into the wilderness, where she hath a place prepared by God, so that there they may nourish her for a thousand two hundred sixty days.

  2. Exodus 19:4 shared image

    God says He bore Israel “as upon eagles’ wings” after dealing with the Egyptians, as the woman is given “the two wings of the great eagle” to escape. Exodus uses the wings as a figure of God’s carrying His people, not as a gift of wings to fly into the wilderness.

    Read matching passage

    Exodus 19:4 ‘You yourselves have seen all that I have done to the Egyptians, and I bore you as upon eagles’ wings, and brought you to Myself.

  3. Daniel 7:25 verbal allusion

    Daniel gives the same period, “a time, times, and half a time”, for which everything is handed into the hands of the one who wears down the holy ones of the Most High. Revelation uses it for the time the woman is nourished away from the serpent.

    Read matching passage

    Daniel 7:25 He will speak words against the Most High, wear down the holy ones of the Most High, and expect to alter seasons and law. Everything will be handed into his hands for a time, times, and half a time.

Revelation 12:15

  1. Isaiah 8:7 shared image

    Isaiah pictures “the river’s strong and abundant water” brought up against a people to overrun them, as the serpent sends water “like a river” to sweep the woman away. In Isaiah the Lord brings the river and identifies it as the king of the Assyrians.

    Read matching passage

  1. Numbers 26:10 verbal allusion

    Both say that “the earth opened its mouth and swallowed.” In Numbers the earth swallows Korah’s company in judgment; in Revelation it swallows the river the dragon sends against the woman, so the earth acts to rescue.

    Numbers 26:10 And the earth opened its mouth and swallowed them and Korah when his congregation died, when the fire consumed the two hundred fifty; they became a sign.

  2. Deuteronomy 11:6 verbal allusion

    Deuteronomy recalls “when the earth opened her mouth and swallowed” Dathan and Abiram; Revelation uses the same image of the earth opening its mouth to swallow, here to protect the woman rather than to punish rebels.

    Deuteronomy 11:6 and what He did unto Dathan and Abiram, the sons of Eliab son of Reuben, when the earth opened her mouth and swallowed them, and their houses, and their tents, and all their substance that was with them, in the midst of all Israel.

    Both visions see beasts rising out of the sea: Daniel’s “four beasts came up from the sea,” and John’s “beast coming up from the sea.” Daniel sees four distinct beasts; Revelation describes one.

    The dragon of 12:3 has “seven heads and ten horns” with diadems, and the beast from the sea has the same “ten horns and seven heads,” its diadems on the horns rather than the heads. 13:2 then says the dragon gives the beast his power.

    Revelation 12:3 And another sign was seen in heaven, and behold, a great fiery red dragon, having seven heads and ten horns, and upon his heads seven diadems.

    Revelation 13:4 restates the transfer: worship is given to the dragon “because he gave authority to the beast,” echoing “the dragon gave it his power and his throne and great authority.”

    Revelation 12:9 identifies “the great dragon” as “the ancient serpent, who is called the Devil and Satan,” naming who gives the beast its power and authority in 13:2.

    Revelation 13:2 narrates what 13:4 summarises: “the dragon gave it his power and his throne and great authority.”

Revelation 13:5

And a mouth speaking great things and blasphemies was given to it, and authority to act for forty-two months was given to it.

  1. Daniel 7:8 verbal allusion

    Daniel’s little horn has “a mouth speaking great things,” the same phrase used for the beast’s mouth. Daniel’s horn sprouts on the fourth beast; in Revelation the mouth is given to the beast itself.

    Read matching passage

    Daniel 7:8 and many plans were in its horns. Another single horn sprouted among them, small among its horns, and three of the earlier horns were withered through it. This horn had eyes like human eyes and a mouth speaking great things, and it made war against the holy ones.

    Pinned target record

  2. Daniel 7:25 shared image

    Daniel’s figure “will speak words against the Most High” and is given power “for a time, times, and half a time,” paralleling the beast’s blasphemies and its limited “forty-two months.” The two periods are expressed differently in the two texts.

    Read matching passage

    Daniel 7:25 He will speak words against the Most High, wear down the holy ones of the Most High, and expect to alter seasons and law. Everything will be handed into his hands for a time, times, and half a time.

  3. Daniel 11:36 shared image

    Daniel’s king “will speak extraordinary things against the God of gods” and prosper for a set time, as the beast is given a mouth for “blasphemies” and authority for a fixed period.

    Read matching passage

    Daniel 11:36 The king will act according to his will, become enraged, and be exalted above every god. He will speak extraordinary things against the God of gods and prosper until the wrath is completed, for completion is coming upon him.

Revelation 13:6

And it opened its mouth in blasphemies against God, to blaspheme His name and His tent, those dwelling in heaven.

  1. Revelation 12:12 verbal echo

    Revelation 12:12 calls on the “heavens and those dwelling in them” to rejoice, while in 13:6 the beast blasphemes “those dwelling in heaven.” Both concern those in heaven, beyond the reach of the dragon’s fury on earth.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 12:12 For this reason rejoice, heavens and those dwelling in them. Woe to the earth and the sea, because the devil came down to you, having great fury, knowing that he hath a little time.”

    Pinned target record

  2. Revelation 21:3 shared image

    The beast blasphemes God’s “tent,” and Revelation 21:3 announces “the tabernacle of God” dwelling with men. The Saints texts use “tent” and “tabernacle” for these, and 21:3 places God’s dwelling among people rather than in heaven.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 21:3 And I heard a great voice from the throne, saying, “Behold, the tabernacle of God is with men, and He shall dwell with them, and they shall be His peoples, and God Himself shall be with them as their God,

    Both use the fourfold formula of tribe, people, tongue and nation: 5:9 has “every tribe and tongue and people and nation,” and 13:7 has “every tribe and people and tongue and nation.” In 5:9 the Lamb purchases people from them for God; in 13:7 the beast receives authority over them.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 5:9 And they sing a new song, saying, “Thou art worthy to take the scroll and open its seals, because Thou wast slaughtered and didst purchase for God in Thy blood people from every tribe and tongue and people and nation,

Revelation 13:8

And all those dwelling upon the earth will worship it, each whose name hath not been written in the book of life of the Lamb slaughtered from the foundation of the world.

  1. Revelation 17:8 parallel description

    Revelation 17:8 repeats that “those dwelling upon the earth, whose name hath not been written in the book of life from the foundation of the world” respond to the beast, there by marvelling rather than worshipping.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 17:8 The beast thou sawest was and is not, and is about to come up from the abyss, and goeth into destruction. And those dwelling upon the earth, whose name hath not been written in the book of life from the foundation of the world, will marvel when they see the beast, because it was and is not and will be present.

  2. Revelation 21:27 same title

    Both speak of the book of life as belonging to the Lamb, “the Lamb’s book of life” in 21:27. There those written in it enter the city; in 13:8 those not written in it worship the beast.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 21:27 And nothing common, and no one practicing abomination and falsehood, will ever enter it, but only those written in the Lamb’s book of life.

    Pinned target record

    Peter speaks of the blood of Christ “as of a lamb” who was foreknown “before the foundation of the world,” connecting the slaughtered Lamb with the foundation of the world as 13:8 does.

    Read matching passage

    1 Peter 1:19 but with precious blood, as of a lamb without blemish and without spot—the blood of Christ,

    1 Peter 1:20 who was foreknown before the foundation of the world but was manifested at the end of the times for your sake.

Revelation 13:10

If anyone is for captivity, into captivity he goeth; if anyone is to be killed by a sword, by a sword he must be killed. Here is the endurance and the faith of the saints.

  1. Jeremiah 15:2 verbal allusion

    Jeremiah’s oracle has the same pattern: “as many for sword, to sword … as many for captivity, to captivity.” Jeremiah lists death and famine too and announces judgment, while Revelation applies the pattern to the saints’ endurance.

    Read matching passage

    Jeremiah 15:2 And if they say to you, ‘Where shall we go out?’ you will say to them: Thus says the Lord: As many as are for death, to death; as many for sword, to sword; as many for famine, to famine; as many for captivity, to captivity.

    Pinned target record

  2. Jeremiah 50:11 verbal allusion

    Jeremiah repeats the formula against Egypt: “those for deportation, to deportation; and those for the sword, to the sword,” the pattern Revelation uses for captivity and the sword.

    Read matching passage

    Jeremiah 50:11 He will enter and strike Egypt’s land: those for death, to death; those for deportation, to deportation; and those for the sword, to the sword.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 14:12 Here is the endurance of the saints, those keeping the commandments of God and the faith of Jesus.

Revelation 13:13

And it performeth great signs, so that it even maketh fire come down from heaven to the earth before people.

  1. 2 Kings 1:10–1:14 verbal echo

    In the Elijah story “fire came down from heaven” and consumed the king’s men, the sign the second beast imitates when it makes “fire come down from heaven to the earth.” Elijah acts as a man of God; the beast performs the sign to deceive.

    Read matching passage

    2 Kings 1:10 Elijah answered and said to the captain of fifty, ‘If I am a man of God, fire will come down from heaven and consume you and your fifty men.’ Fire came down from heaven and consumed him and his fifty men.

    2 Kings 1:11 The king again sent to him another captain of fifty with his fifty men. The captain went up and spoke to him, saying, ‘Man of God, thus says the king: Come down quickly!’

    2 Kings 1:12 Elijah answered and spoke to him, saying, ‘If I am a man of God, fire will come down from heaven and consume you and your fifty men.’ Fire came down from heaven and consumed him and his fifty men.

    2 Kings 1:13 The king again sent a commander, a third captain of fifty, with his fifty men. The third captain of fifty came to him, bent his knees before Elijah, and pleaded with him, saying, ‘Man of God, please let my life and the lives of these fifty slaves of yours be honored in your eyes.’

    2 Kings 1:14 ‘Behold, fire came down from heaven and consumed the first two captains of fifty and their fifty men. But now please let the lives of your slaves be honored in your eyes.’

    Pinned target record

Revelation 14:10

he too will drink from the wine of God’s fury, prepared unmixed in the cup of His wrath, and will be tormented in fire and sulfur before holy angels and before the Lamb.

  1. Psalms 74:9 verbal allusion

    The psalm says “in the Lord’s hand is a cup of unmixed wine” of which “all the earth’s sinners will drink,” matching the wine “prepared unmixed in the cup of His wrath.” The psalm also speaks of mixture and dregs.

    Read matching passage

    Psalms 74:9 For in the Lord’s hand is a cup of unmixed wine, full of mixture; and He tilted from this into that, but its dregs were not emptied out; all the earth’s sinners will drink.

    Pinned target record

  2. Psalms 10:6 shared image

    The psalm says “fire, sulfur” are “the portion of their cup” for sinners, combining cup, fire and sulfur as 14:10 does.

    Read matching passage

    Psalms 10:6 He will rain traps upon sinners; fire, sulfur, and a storm wind are the portion of their cup.

    Pinned target record

  3. Revelation 16:19 verbal parallel

    Revelation 16:19 speaks of “the cup of the wine of the fury of His wrath” given to Babylon, the same wording as in 14:10, applied to the city rather than to individual worshippers.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 16:19 And the great city became three parts, and the cities of the nations fell. And Babylon the great was remembered before God, to give her the cup of the wine of the fury of His wrath.

    Pinned target record

Revelation 14:11

And the smoke of their torment goeth up for ages of ages, and they have no rest day and night, those worshipping the beast and its image, and anyone who receiveth the mark of its name.”

  1. Isaiah 34:10 verbal allusion

    Isaiah says of Edom’s land that its burning will continue “night and day” and “her smoke will rise upward” for an everlasting time, the image applied to the beast’s worshippers.

    Read matching passage

    Isaiah 34:10 night and day. It will not be quenched for an everlasting time, and her smoke will rise upward. For generations and a long time she will be deserted.

    Pinned target record

  2. Revelation 19:3 verbal parallel

    Revelation 19:3 says of Babylon, “her smoke goeth up for the ages of the ages,” the same expression used in 14:11.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 19:3 And a second time they said, “Hallelujah! And her smoke goeth up for the ages of the ages.”

    Pinned target record

  3. Revelation 20:10 parallel judgment

    Revelation 20:10 says the devil, the beast and the false prophet “will be tormented day and night for the ages of the ages,” matching the unceasing torment “day and night” in 14:11.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 20:10 And the devil who deceived them was thrown into the lake of fire and sulfur, where also are the beast and the false prophet, and they will be tormented day and night for the ages of the ages.

    Pinned target record

Revelation 14:12

Here is the endurance of the saints, those keeping the commandments of God and the faith of Jesus.

  1. Revelation 13:10 repeated formula

    Revelation 13:10 ends with the same formula, “Here is the endurance and the faith of the saints.”

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 13:10 If anyone is for captivity, into captivity he goeth; if anyone is to be killed by a sword, by a sword he must be killed. Here is the endurance and the faith of the saints.

    Pinned target record

  2. Revelation 12:17 verbal parallel

    Revelation 12:17 describes the woman’s offspring as “those keeping the commandments of God and holding the testimony of Jesus,” the same description of the saints with “testimony” in place of “faith.”

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 12:17 And the dragon was enraged at the woman, and went away to make war with the rest of her offspring, those keeping the commandments of God and holding the testimony of Jesus.

    Pinned target record

Revelation 14:13

And I heard a voice from heaven saying, “Write: Blessed are the dead who die in the Lord from now on.” “Yea,” saith the Spirit, “so that they may rest from their labours, for their works follow with them.”

  1. Revelation 19:9 repeated formula

    Revelation 19:9 has the same command and beatitude form, “Write: Blessed are …,” there for those called to the Lamb’s marriage supper.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 19:9 And he saith unto me, “Write: Blessed are those called to the supper of the Lamb’s marriage.” And he saith unto me, “These are true words of God.”

    Pinned target record

  2. Hebrews 4:9–4:11 verbal echo

    Hebrews says the one who entered God’s rest “rested from his own works,” paralleling the dead who “rest from their labours.” Hebrews speaks of a Sabbath rest that remains for God’s people, not explicitly of the dead.

    Read matching passage

    Hebrews 4:9 So then, a Sabbath rest remains for the people of God.

    Hebrews 4:10 For the one who entered His rest also rested from his own works, just as God did from His own.

    Hebrews 4:11 Therefore let us be diligent to enter that rest, so that no one may fall in the same pattern of disobedience.

    Pinned target record

  3. Wisdom of Solomon 4:7 shared image

    Wisdom says that a righteous person who dies early “will be at rest,” the same rest promised to the dead who die in the Lord.

    Read matching passage

    Wisdom of Solomon 4:7 But if a righteous person dies early, he will be at rest.

    Pinned target record

Revelation 14:14

And I saw, and behold, a white cloud, and upon the cloud One sitting like a son of man, having upon His head a golden crown and in His hand a sharp sickle.

  1. Daniel 7:13 verbal allusion

    Daniel sees “upon the clouds of heaven, one like a son of man,” the figure John sees sitting “upon the cloud … like a son of man.” Daniel does not mention a crown or a sickle.

    Read matching passage

    Daniel 7:13 I watched in the night vision, and there, upon the clouds of heaven, one like a son of man was coming. He was present as an Ancient of Days, and those standing by were present with him.

    Pinned target record

  2. Revelation 1:13 same title of Christ

    Revelation 1:13 describes “One like a son of man” among the lampstands, using the same title for the figure who appears here with a golden crown and sickle.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 1:13 and in the midst of the lampstands One like a son of man, clothed in a robe reaching to the feet and girded around the chest with a golden belt.

    Pinned target record

  3. Joel 4:12–4:13 shared image

    Joel calls, “Send out sickles,” where the Lord will sit to judge the nations, the harvest-judgment imagery behind the sickle in the hand of the One on the cloud.

    Read matching passage

    Joel 4:12 Let all the nations be roused and come up to the Valley of Jehoshaphat, because there I will sit to judge all the surrounding nations.

    Joel 4:13 Send out sickles, because the vintage is at hand! Enter and tread, because the winepress is full; the vats overflow, because their evils have multiplied.

    Pinned target record

Revelation 14:15

And another angel came out of the temple, crying with a loud voice to the One sitting upon the cloud, “Send Thy sickle and reap, because the hour to reap hath come, because the harvest of the earth hath dried.”

  1. Joel 4:13 verbal allusion

    Joel’s command “Send out sickles, because the vintage is at hand” is the pattern for “Send Thy sickle and reap, because the hour to reap hath come.” Joel speaks of the vintage and winepress, which Revelation takes up in 14:18–20.

    Read matching passage

    Joel 4:13 Send out sickles, because the vintage is at hand! Enter and tread, because the winepress is full; the vats overflow, because their evils have multiplied.

    Pinned target record

  2. Mark 4:29 verbal echo

    In the parable the farmer “sends in the sickle, because the harvest has arrived,” using the same sequence of sickle and ripe harvest. The parable concerns the growth of the kingdom rather than judgment.

    Read matching passage

    Mark 4:29 When the crop is ready, he immediately sends in the sickle, because the harvest has arrived.”

    Pinned target record

  3. Matthew 13:39 shared image

    Jesus explains that “the harvest is the consummation of the age” and “the reapers are angels,” the end-time harvest image in which an angel here calls for the reaping.

    Read matching passage

    Matthew 13:39 the enemy who sowed them is the devil; the harvest is the consummation of the age; and the reapers are angels.

    Pinned target record

Revelation 14:16

And the One sitting upon the cloud cast His sickle upon the earth, and the earth was reaped.

  1. Revelation 14:14 internal cross-reference

    Revelation 14:14 introduces the “One sitting upon the cloud” holding “a sharp sickle,” the figure who now casts the sickle on the earth.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 14:14 And I saw, and behold, a white cloud, and upon the cloud One sitting like a son of man, having upon His head a golden crown and in His hand a sharp sickle.

    Pinned target record

  2. Revelation 14:19 parallel action

    Revelation 14:19 repeats the action with an angel who “cast his sickle into the earth,” this time gathering the vine for the winepress of God’s fury.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 14:19 And the angel cast his sickle into the earth and gathered the vine of the earth, and threw it into the great winepress of God’s fury.

    Pinned target record

Revelation 14:17

And another angel came out of the temple in heaven, himself also having a sharp sickle.

  1. Revelation 14:14–14:15 internal cross-reference

    Revelation 14:14–15 shows the One on the cloud with “a sharp sickle” and an angel coming “out of the temple”; 14:17 introduces another angel from the temple, “himself also having a sharp sickle.”

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 14:14 And I saw, and behold, a white cloud, and upon the cloud One sitting like a son of man, having upon His head a golden crown and in His hand a sharp sickle.

    Revelation 14:15 And another angel came out of the temple, crying with a loud voice to the One sitting upon the cloud, “Send Thy sickle and reap, because the hour to reap hath come, because the harvest of the earth hath dried.”

    Pinned target record

  2. Revelation 14:18 internal cross-reference

    Revelation 14:18 addresses this angel as “the one having the sharp sickle” and commands him to gather the clusters of the vine.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 14:18 And another angel came from the altar, the one having authority over the fire, and called with a loud voice to the one having the sharp sickle, saying, “Send thy sharp sickle and gather the clusters of the vine of the earth, because its grapes have ripened.”

    Pinned target record

Revelation 14:18

And another angel came from the altar, the one having authority over the fire, and called with a loud voice to the one having the sharp sickle, saying, “Send thy sharp sickle and gather the clusters of the vine of the earth, because its grapes have ripened.”

  1. Joel 4:13 verbal allusion

    Joel’s “Send out sickles, because the vintage is at hand!” lies behind “Send thy sharp sickle and gather the clusters … because its grapes have ripened.” Joel goes on to the trodden winepress, as Revelation does in 14:19–20.

    Read matching passage

    Joel 4:13 Send out sickles, because the vintage is at hand! Enter and tread, because the winepress is full; the vats overflow, because their evils have multiplied.

    Pinned target record

  2. Revelation 14:15–14:16 parallel vision

    Revelation 14:15–16 has the same pattern: an angel cries “with a loud voice” to the one with the sickle, “Send Thy sickle and reap,” and the earth is reaped. The first is a reaping of “the harvest of the earth”; the second is a gathering of “the clusters of the vine of the earth.”

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 14:15 And another angel came out of the temple, crying with a loud voice to the One sitting upon the cloud, “Send Thy sickle and reap, because the hour to reap hath come, because the harvest of the earth hath dried.”

    Revelation 14:16 And the One sitting upon the cloud cast His sickle upon the earth, and the earth was reaped.

    Pinned target record

Revelation 14:19

And the angel cast his sickle into the earth and gathered the vine of the earth, and threw it into the great winepress of God’s fury.

  1. Deuteronomy 32:32–32:33 shared image

    Deuteronomy speaks of a wicked people’s “vine” as “of the vine of Sodom,” with grapes of gall and a bitter “cluster,” the image of a corrupt vine taken up in “the vine of the earth.” Deuteronomy describes the vine’s fruit, not its harvest or a winepress.

    Read matching passage

    Deuteronomy 32:32 For their vine is of the vine of Sodom, and their tendril is of Gomorrah; their grape is a grape of gall, their cluster is bitterness.

    Deuteronomy 32:33 Their wine is the venom of serpents, and the incurable venom of asps.

    Pinned target record

Revelation 14:20

And the winepress was trodden outside the city, and blood came out of the winepress as far as the horses’ bridles, for a thousand six hundred stadia.

  1. Isaiah 63:1–63:3 shared image

    In Isaiah the Lord’s garments are red as from “a trodden winepress”: He trampled the nations “in wrath” and “brought their blood down to earth.” This is the image of a winepress of judgment yielding blood, as in 14:20.

    Read matching passage

    Isaiah 63:1 Who is this arriving from Edom, with redness of garments from Bosor, so beautiful in His robe, with force and strength? “I speak righteousness and saving judgment.”

    Isaiah 63:2 Why are Thy garments red and Thy clothes as though from a trodden winepress?

    Isaiah 63:3 “I am full of what has been trodden. No man from the nations is with Me. I trampled them in wrath, crushed them like earth, and brought their blood down to earth.

    Pinned target record

  2. Lamentations 1:15 shared image

    Lamentations says “The Lord trod a winepress” against Daughter Judah, using the treading of the winepress as a picture of God’s judgment. There it falls on Judah, not on the nations.

    Read matching passage

    Lamentations 1:15 The Lord removed all my strong men from my midst. He called a time against me to crush my chosen men. The Lord trod a winepress for the virgin Daughter Judah; for these things I weep.

    Pinned target record
