Revelation 15:5

And after these things I saw, and the temple of the tent of the testimony in heaven was opened,

  1. Revelation 11:19 parallel vision

    Both verses describe the temple in heaven being “opened.” In 11:19 the ark of His covenant is then seen; here the opening leads to the coming out of the seven angels with the seven plagues.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 11:19 And God’s temple in heaven was opened, and the ark of His covenant was seen in His temple; and there were lightnings and voices and thunders and an earthquake and great hail.

    Pinned target record

  2. Numbers 1:50 shared title

    Revelation calls the heavenly sanctuary “the temple of the tent of the testimony,” using the same title Numbers gives the wilderness “Tent of Testimony” served by the Levites. Numbers speaks of the earthly tent and its furnishings, not a temple in heaven.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 1:50 And you shall appoint the Levites over the Tent of Testimony, and over all its furnishings, and over all that is therein. They shall carry the Tent and all its furnishings, and they shall minister therein, and shall camp round about the Tent.

    Pinned target record

Revelation 15:6

and the seven angels having the seven plagues came out of the temple, clothed in pure, bright linen and girded around their chests with golden belts.

  1. Revelation 1:13 shared image

    The angels are “girded around their chests with golden belts,” as the One like a son of man is “girded around the chest with a golden belt.” The angels wear pure, bright linen, whereas the son of man wears a robe reaching to the feet.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 1:13 and in the midst of the lampstands One like a son of man, clothed in a robe reaching to the feet and girded around the chest with a golden belt.

    Pinned target record

  2. Revelation 15:1 internal cross-reference

    Both verses speak of the “seven angels having seven plagues.” 15:1 introduces them as a sign in heaven; 15:6 shows them coming out of the temple.

Revelation 15:7

And one of the four living creatures gave the seven angels seven golden bowls full of the fury of God, who liveth for the ages of the ages.

  1. Revelation 21:9 internal cross-reference

    Both verses speak of the seven angels with seven bowls full of judgement: here “seven golden bowls full of the fury of God,” there “the seven bowls full of the seven last plagues.” In 21:9 one of these angels says to the seer, “Come, I will show thee the bride, the wife of the Lamb.”

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 21:9 And one of the seven angels having the seven bowls full of the seven last plagues came and spoke with me, saying, “Come, I will show thee the bride, the wife of the Lamb.”

    Pinned target record

  2. Revelation 5:8 verbal parallel

    Both scenes pair the four living creatures with “golden bowls full of” something. In 5:8 the bowls are full of incense, “the prayers of the saints,” whereas here they are full of “the fury of God.”

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 5:8 And when He took the scroll, the four living creatures and the twenty-four elders fell before the Lamb, each having a lyre and golden bowls full of incense, which are the prayers of the saints.

    Pinned target record

  3. Revelation 4:6–4:9 same divine title

    The throne vision introduces the four living creatures and describes God as “the One living for the ages of the ages”; 15:7 uses the same description of God and has one of the four living creatures hand over the bowls.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 4:6 And before the throne was something like a glassy sea, like crystal. And in the midst of the throne and around the throne were four living creatures, full of eyes in front and behind.

    Revelation 4:7 And the first living creature was like a lion, and the second living creature like a calf, and the third living creature had a face like a human being’s, and the fourth living creature was like an eagle flying.

    Revelation 4:8 And the four living creatures, each one of them having six wings, are full of eyes around and within; and they have no rest day and night, saying, “Holy, holy, holy, Lord God Almighty, the One who was and who is and who is coming.”

    Revelation 4:9 And whenever the living creatures give glory and honour and thanksgiving to the One sitting upon the throne, the One living for the ages of the ages,

Revelation 15:8

And the temple was filled with smoke from the glory of God and from His power, and no one was able to enter the temple until the seven plagues of the seven angels were completed.

  1. Exodus 40:34–40:35 shared image

    In both, the sanctuary is filled with the glory of God and no one can go in: “Moses could not enter the Tent of Testimony” because the tent “was filled with the glory of the Lord.” Exodus speaks of a cloud; Revelation speaks of smoke and adds that entry is barred until the seven plagues are completed.

    Read matching passage

    Exodus 40:34 The cloud covered the Tent of Testimony, and the tent was filled with the glory of the Lord.

    Exodus 40:35 Moses could not enter the Tent of Testimony because the cloud overshadowed it and the tent was filled with the glory of the Lord.

    Pinned target record

  2. Isaiah 6:4 shared image

    In both visions the sanctuary is filled with smoke: “the house was filled with smoke” and “the temple was filled with smoke.” In Isaiah this happens as the lintel is lifted “at the sound of their cry,” while Revelation says the smoke comes “from the glory of God and from His power.”

    Read matching passage

    Isaiah 6:4 And the lintel was lifted at the sound of their cry, and the house was filled with smoke.

    Pinned target record

  3. 1 Kings 8:10–8:11 shared image

    At the temple’s dedication the Lord’s glory filled the house and the priests “could not stand to minister,” just as in Revelation no one could enter the temple. Kings speaks of a cloud rather than smoke.

    Read matching passage

    1 Kings 8:10 When the priests came out of the Holy Place, the cloud filled the house.

    1 Kings 8:11 The priests could not stand to minister because of the cloud, for the Lord’s glory filled the house.

    Pinned target record

Revelation 16:1

And I heard a loud voice from the temple saying to the seven angels, “Go and pour out the seven bowls of God’s fury into the earth.”

  1. Revelation 15:5–15:8 internal cross-reference

    The voice sends out the seven angels who in 15:5–8 came out of the temple and received “seven golden bowls full of the fury of God.” 16:1 orders that these bowls now be poured out on the earth.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 15:5 And after these things I saw, and the temple of the tent of the testimony in heaven was opened,

    Revelation 15:6 and the seven angels having the seven plagues came out of the temple, clothed in pure, bright linen and girded around their chests with golden belts.

    Revelation 15:7 And one of the four living creatures gave the seven angels seven golden bowls full of the fury of God, who liveth for the ages of the ages.

    Revelation 15:8 And the temple was filled with smoke from the glory of God and from His power, and no one was able to enter the temple until the seven plagues of the seven angels were completed.

    Pinned target record

  2. Revelation 16:17 internal cross-reference

    The bowl sequence opens and closes with “a loud voice” from the temple. In 16:1 it orders the bowls poured out; in 16:17, after the seventh bowl, it declares “It hath happened.”

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 16:17 And the seventh poured out his bowl upon the air, and a loud voice came out of the temple from the throne, saying, “It hath happened.”

    Pinned target record

  3. Jeremiah 10:25 verbal allusion

    Jeremiah prays, “Pour Thy fury upon nations,” and Revelation commands the angels to “pour out the seven bowls of God’s fury into the earth.” Jeremiah asks this against nations that devoured Jacob, while Revelation’s command extends to the earth.

    Read matching passage

    Jeremiah 10:25 Pour Thy fury upon nations who do not know Thee and upon generations who did not call on Thy name, because they devoured Jacob and consumed him and made his pasture desolate.

    Pinned target record

Revelation 16:2

And the first went and poured out his bowl into the earth, and a bad and painful sore came upon the people having the mark of the beast and worshipping its image.

  1. Exodus 9:8–9:11 shared image

    Like the Egyptian plague in which boils broke out “on people,” the first bowl brings a painful sore upon people. In Exodus the boils come from scattered furnace soot and fall on Egypt’s people and animals, while in Revelation the sore falls on those bearing the beast’s mark.

    Read matching passage

    Exodus 9:8 The Lord said to Moses and Aaron, “Take handfuls of soot from a furnace, and let Moses scatter it toward heaven before Pharaoh and his attendants.

    Exodus 9:9 Let it become fine dust over all the land of Egypt. Boils, with blisters breaking out, shall come upon people and four-footed animals, on people and four-footed animals throughout Egypt.”

    Exodus 9:10 He took soot from the furnace before Pharaoh. Moses scattered it toward heaven, and boils with blisters broke out on people and four-footed animals.

    Exodus 9:11 The sorcerers could not stand before Moses because of the boils, for the boils were on the sorcerers and throughout the land of Egypt.

    Pinned target record

  2. Revelation 16:11 internal cross-reference

    The sores of the first bowl are mentioned again after the fifth, when people blaspheme God “because of their pains and their sores” and do not repent.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 16:11 and blasphemed the God of heaven because of their pains and their sores, and did not repent of their works.

    Pinned target record

  3. Revelation 13:15–13:18 internal cross-reference

    The sore falls on “the people having the mark of the beast and worshipping its image,” the people described in 13:15–17, where the beast’s image is worshipped and all are made to receive the mark.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 13:15 And it was given to it to give breath to the beast’s image, so that the beast’s image might both speak and cause as many as would not worship the beast’s image to be killed.

    Revelation 13:16 And it causeth all—the small and the great, and the rich and the poor, and the free and the slaves—to be given a mark upon their right hand or upon their forehead,

    Revelation 13:17 so that no one may be able to buy or sell except the one having the mark, the name of the beast or the number of its name.

    Revelation 13:18 Here is wisdom. Let the one who hath understanding calculate the number of the beast, for it is a human number, and its number is six hundred sixty-six.

    Pinned target record

Revelation 16:3

And the second poured out his bowl into the sea, and it became blood like a dead person’s, and every living soul died, the things in the sea.

  1. Revelation 8:8–8:9 parallel judgment

    The second trumpet and the second bowl both strike the sea: it “became blood” and creatures in the sea died. The trumpet affects a third of the sea and its creatures, while the bowl kills “every living soul” in the sea.

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 8:8 And the second angel sounded, and something like a great mountain burning with fire was thrown into the sea; and a third of the sea became blood,

    Revelation 8:9 and a third of the creatures in the sea, those having life, died, and a third of the ships were destroyed.

    Pinned target record

  2. Exodus 7:17–7:21 shared image

    Water turned to blood and the death of the creatures in it recall the Egyptian plague, where “all the water in the river turned to blood” and “the fish in the river died.” Exodus concerns Egypt’s river and waters, while Revelation concerns the sea.

    Read matching passage

    Exodus 7:17 Thus says the Lord: “By this you shall know that I am the Lord: behold, I strike with the staff in My hand upon the water in the river, and it shall be turned to blood.

    Exodus 7:18 The fish in the river will die, the river will stink, and the Egyptians will be unable to drink its water.”’”

    Exodus 7:19 And the Lord said to Moses, “Say to Aaron your brother, ‘Take your staff, and stretch out your hand over the waters of Egypt, over their rivers, and over their canals, and over their marshes, and over all their standing water, and it shall become blood.’” And there was blood throughout all the land of Egypt, both in wood and in stone.

    Exodus 7:20 Moses and Aaron did as the Lord commanded. He raised his staff and struck the river’s water before Pharaoh and his attendants, and all the water in the river turned to blood.

    Exodus 7:21 The fish in the river died, and the river stank. The Egyptians could not drink its water, and there was blood throughout the land of Egypt.

  1. Exodus 7:17–7:20 shared image

    Judges describes kings who “drew up in battle” and fought “by Megiddo’s water,” a battle of kings at the place whose name resembles Harmagedon. Revelation gives only the Hebrew name and does not explain it.

    2 Kings 23:30 His servants transported him dead from Mageddo, brought him to Jerusalem, and buried him in his grave in David’s city. The people of the land took Joachas son of Josiah, anointed him, and made him king in his father’s place.

Revelation 16:21

And great hail, about a talent in weight, cometh down from heaven upon people; and people blasphemed God because of the plague of hail, because its plague is exceedingly great.

  1. Revelation 11:19 parallel sequence

    The seventh trumpet sequence ends with “an earthquake and great hail,” as the seventh bowl brings a great earthquake (16:18) followed by “great hail.”

    Read matching passage

    Revelation 11:19 And God’s temple in heaven was opened, and the ark of His covenant was seen in His temple; and there were lightnings and voices and thunders and an earthquake and great hail.

    Pinned target record

  2. Joshua 10:11 shared image

    In both, hail comes down “from heaven” in judgment. In Joshua the Lord hurls hailstones from heaven on the fleeing armies, and more die from the hailstones than by the sword; in Revelation great hail, about a talent in weight, comes down from heaven upon people, who then blaspheme God because of the plague of hail.

    Read matching passage

    Joshua 10:11 As they fled before the sons of Israel down the descent of Horonin, the Lord hurled hailstones from heaven upon them as far as Azekah. More died from the hailstones than the sons of Israel killed with the sword in battle.

    Pinned target record

  3. Ezekiel 38:21–38:22 shared image

    God’s judgment on Gog includes “hailstones,” as the final bowl brings great hail. Ezekiel also lists fire and sulfur, which Revelation does not mention here.
